<!-- head-1: body_md -->
In this work, we identify a new high-dimensional phenomenon in convex optimization and achieve the $O( 1 / T)$ rate mentioned in the $(1, 2)$ example above, up to log factors. More generally, for the $(p, q)$-norm case our rates are
\[
{\protect\hyperlink{def:big_O_p}{\normalcolor \widetilde{O}_{p, q}}}\left( \frac{1}{ T^{ \frac{1}{p} - \left( \frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2} \right)_+
        }} \right),
\] 
which nearly match the mentioned lower bounds up to logarithmic factors, where $(x)_+ = \max\{x, 0\}$, $1 / \infty = 0$, solving the nonsmooth part of the COLT 2015 open question in \citep{pmlr-v40-Guzman15},
providing a polynomial improvement over the rate of Mirror Descent algorithms $O( T^{ -1 / \max\{q, 2\}} )=O( 1  / T^{1/q - (1/q - 1/2)_+ } )$, when $p < \min\{q, 2\}$. Additionally, we obtain an upper bound in the general setting where the feasible set $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$ and the set of possible subgradients $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$ are convex and centrally symmetric, showing that the minimax complexity is upper bounded by a combinatorial quantity known as the 
\emph{sequential fat-shattering dimension} (sfat) which is no less than the fat-shattering dimension (fat), and we show they are the same up to log factors in the $(p, q)$-case. This general result provides the first positive contribution towards understanding the question posed by \citet{sridharan2012learning}.  
We achieve our results by defining and studying a new online learning game, of independent interest, which we explain in the following.
<!-- end head-1 -->
<!-- head-2: body_md -->
An intermediate expression to bound the max-regret minimax value in the $(p, q)$-norm case, if specialized to the case where the losses are linear and iid, results in a problem of independent interest about how fast the convex hull of samples of a fixed distribution supported on $B_{q^\ast}$, approaches its mean in $\ell_{p^\ast}$ distance, cf. \cref{lem:iid-online-reduction-identity,eq:worst-case-iid-convex-hull-distance}. Or a more general statement when working with general sets $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$, $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$.
Our techniques also yield upper and lower bounds for this problem, see \cref{thm:iid-centered-convex-hull,prop:iid-high-dimensional-instantiation}, which match in the $(p, q)$-norm case.
This result can be viewed as an early-time, quantitative analogue of Wendel-type convex-hull containment theorems \citep{wendel1962problem}: the latter gives the exact probability that the convex hull of iid points from a centrally symmetric distribution in general position contains the origin, and gives a sharp transition at around the dimension. We instead measure the distance of the convex hull to the mean in the $\ell_{p^\ast}$ norm and we use general \emph{bounded} distributions, rather than symmetric ones. Previous quantitative variants have been obtained in very specific cases, including Euclidean distance for the uniform distribution \citep{liu2014probabilities} and distribution-dependent Euclidean-distance settings \citep{hayakawa2023estimating}.
<!-- end head-2 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def:fat-shattering-dimensions}
For a set $\mathcal{Z}$, let \(\mathcal G\subseteq\mathbb R^{\mathcal Z}\) and $\alpha>0$.
A set
\(z_1,\dots,z_m\in\mathcal Z\) is \(\alpha\)-shattered by \(\mathcal G\) if
there are thresholds \(s_1,\dots,s_m\in\mathbb R\) such that, for every
\(\varepsilon\in\{\pm1\}^m\), some \(h_\varepsilon\in\mathcal G\) satisfies
\[
    \varepsilon_i\bigl(h_\varepsilon(z_i)-s_i\bigr)\ge\frac{\alpha}{2},
    \qquad i=1,\dots,m.
\]
 
A depth-\(T\) predictable tree over \(\mathcal Z\) is a sequence
\(\mathbf z=(z_t)_{t=1}^T\), with
\(z_t:\{\pm1\}^{t-1}\to\mathcal Z\).  It is sequentially
\(\alpha\)-shattered by \(\mathcal G\) if there is a real-valued predictable
tree \(\mathbf s=(s_t)_{t=1}^T\) such that, for every
\(\varepsilon\in\{\pm1\}^T\), some \(h_\varepsilon\in\mathcal G\) satisfies
\[
    \varepsilon_t
    \bigl(h_\varepsilon(z_t(\varepsilon_{<t}))
    -s_t(\varepsilon_{<t})\bigr)
    \ge \frac{\alpha}{2},
    \qquad t=1,\dots,T.
\]
The fat-shattering dimension \( 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        \llap{\smash{ 
        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
            \setlength\@tempdima\HyperRaiseLinkDefault
            \HyperRaiseLinkHook
        \expandafter\endgroup
        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{ 
            \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
            \hypertarget{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{} 
            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha](\mathcal G)\) and its sequential version \( 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        \llap{\smash{ 
        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
            \setlength\@tempdima\HyperRaiseLinkDefault
            \HyperRaiseLinkHook
        \expandafter\endgroup
        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{ 
            \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
            \hypertarget{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{} 
            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha](\mathcal G)\) are the largest possible such \(m\) and $T$, respectively.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{def:sequential-cover}
For a fixed depth-$T$ \(\mathcal Z\)-valued tree \(\mathbf z\), an
    $\ell_\infty$ sequential \(\alpha\)-cover $\mathcal V_{\mathbf z}$ of \(\mathcal G\)  is a set of depth-\(T\) trees over $\mathbb{R}$ such that
\[
    \forall h\in\mathcal G,\ \forall \varepsilon\in\{\pm1\}^T,\
    \exists \mathbf v\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathbf z}\ \text{ such that }\
    \max_{1\le t\le T}
    \left|
        h\bigl(z_t(\varepsilon_{<t})\bigr)
        -
        v_t(\varepsilon_{<t})
    \right|
    \le \alpha.
\]
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- assumption-1: body_md -->
\label{ass:minimax_theorem_consequence}
Let $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
 \subseteq E$, $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
 \subseteq E^{\ast}$ be nonempty, convex and symmetric with respect to $0$, with $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$ bounded and $ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]<\infty$, and such that the minimax theorem consequence $ 
    \IfNoValueTF{$}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};}}{$} } 
 applies, where $r_t$ and $s_t$ are distributions over the affine loss class $\mathcal{F} := \{x \mapsto \langle g, x \rangle + b :(g,b)\in 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:affine-sample-space}{\normalcolor \mathcal Z\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]\}$ and over $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$, chosen by the adversary and the player, respectively. The minimax value of the game is defined as:
 
 
\begin{align} 
      
     
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{def:max-regret-value}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        \llap{\smash{ 
        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
            \setlength\@tempdima\HyperRaiseLinkDefault
            \HyperRaiseLinkHook
        \expandafter\endgroup
        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{ 
            \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
            \hypertarget{def:max-regret-value}{} 
            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T] &:= \left(\inf_{x_t \in  
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}\sup_{\ell_t \in \mathcal{F}}\right)_{t=1}^T \left\{ \sum_{t=1}^T \ell_{t}(x_t) - \inf_{u \in  
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}  T \max_{t\in[T]} \ell_{t}(u) \right\} \label{eqn:max_regret_value} \\
    &\circled{1}[=] \left(\sup_{r_t} \inf_{s_t} \mathbb{E}_{\substack{x_t \sim s_t\\ \ell_t \sim r_t}}\right)_{t=1}^T  \left\{ \sum_{t=1}^T \ell_t(x_t) - \inf_{u \in  
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
} T \max_{t\in[T]} \ell_t(u) \right\}  \notag 
      
\end{align}
    We further assume that for every \(\eta>0\), an \(\eta\)-minimizing action exists for the conditional expectation of an announced loss distribution $\inf_{x\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
} \mathbb E_{\ell_t\sim r_t(\cdot\mid x_{1:t-1},\ell_{1:t-1})} \ell_t(x)$.
<!-- end assumption-1 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lemma:m-online-to-batch}
    For a convex set $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$, let $f: 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
 \to \mathbb{R}$ be a convex function and for an $\max$-regret game \cref{eq:m-regret} with query $x_t$, $t \in [T]$, let $\ell_t(x): 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
 \to \mathbb{R}$ satisfy $\ell_t(x) \leq f(x)$ and $\ell_t(x_t) = f(x_t)$. Then for $\bar{x}_T = T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^T x_t$ and any $u \in  
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$, we have
\[
    f(\bar x_T)-f(u) \le \frac{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret}{\normalcolor R\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T]}{T}.
\] 
For instance, $u$ can be a minimizer $x^\ast$ of $f$, if it exists.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
We use Jensen's inequality in $ 
    \IfNoValueTF{$}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};}}{$} } 
, the assumption about $\ell_t$ in $ 
    \IfNoValueTF{$}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {2};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {2};}}{$} } 
. For $ 
    \IfNoValueTF{$}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {3};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {3};}}{$} } 
, we use the definition of $\max$-regret that compares with the best fixed action versus the fixed action $u$:
\[
\begin{aligned}
f(\bar x_T)-f(u)
    \circled{1}[\le]
\sum_{t=1}^T \frac{f(x_t)}{T}- f(u) 
    \circled{2}[\le]
\sum_{t=1}^T \frac{\ell_t(x_t)}{T}-\max_{t\le T}\ell_t(u) 
    \circled{3}[\le]
\frac{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret}{\normalcolor R\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T]}{T}.
\end{aligned}
\]
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:generalized_triplex_like_theorem}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:thm:generalized_triplex_like_theorem}{$\downarrow$}}]}
{
Under \cref{ass:minimax_theorem_consequence}, let
\(r_t(\cdot\mid\ell_{1:t-1})\) range over predictable distributions for admissible losses, and define
\(
    m_t(u):=
    \int \ell(u)\,r_t(d\ell\mid\ell_{1:t-1}).
\)
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:online_reduced_value_game}
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T]
    \le
    \left(\sup_{r_t}\mathbb E_{\ell_t\sim r_t}\right)\!{\vphantom{\Big|}}_{t=1}^{T}
    \sup_{u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}
    \left\{
        \sum_{t=1}^T m_t(u)
        -T\max_{t\le T}\ell_t(u)
    \right\}.
\end{equation}
}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:iid-online-reduction-identity}
{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:lem:iid-online-reduction-identity}{$\downarrow$}}]}
Let \(P\) be supported on \( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\), with mean \(\mu_P:=\int g\,dP(g)\). If the
losses in the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:online_reduced_value_game} are the
iid linear losses \(\ell_t(x)=\langle G_t,x\rangle\), with
    \(G_{1:T}\stackrel{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}P\) and $m_t(u) = \mathbb{E} \ell_t(u)$, then the corresponding term,
divided by \(T\), satisfies
\[
    \frac{1}{T}
    \mathbb E_{G_{1:T}\stackrel{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}P}
    \sup_{u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}
    \left\{
        \sum_{t=1}^T m_t(u)
        -
        T\max_{t\le T}\langle G_t,u\rangle
    \right\}
    =
    \mathbb E_{G_{1:T}\stackrel{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}P}
    \operatorname{dist}_{ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:polar-body}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[][\circ]}
    \left(\mu_P,\operatorname{conv}\{G_{1:T}\}\right).
\]
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:iid-centered-convex-hull}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:thm:iid-centered-convex-hull}{$\downarrow$}}]}
Let \(T\ge2\), set \(\Lambda:= 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]\), and write
\( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\eta][ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]:=

<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:iid-high-dimensional-instantiation}
{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:prop:iid-high-dimensional-instantiation}{$\downarrow$}}]}
Let \(1\le p,q\le\infty\) and \(T\ge2\). If \(d\ge T\), then
\[
    \left(\frac{d}{T}\right)^{(\frac1q-\frac1p)_+}
    \frac{1}{T^{\frac1p-(\frac1q-\frac12)_+}}
    \lesssim_{p,q}
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:worst-case-convex-hull-distance}{\normalcolor \operatorname{ConvDist}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\mathrm{wc}] 
[p,q^\ast,T]
    =
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:big_O_p}{\normalcolor \widetilde{O}_{p,q}}}\left( 
        \left(\frac{d}{T}\right)^{(\frac1q-\frac1p)_+}
        \frac{1}{T^{\frac1p-(\frac1q-\frac12)_+}}
     \right).
\]
If \(d\le T\), then
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:worst-case-convex-hull-distance}{\normalcolor \operatorname{ConvDist}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\mathrm{wc}] 
[p,q^\ast,T]
    =
    \widetilde{\Theta}_{p,q}\!\left(
        \frac{d^{1-\frac1p+(\frac1q-\frac12)_+}}{T}
    \right).
\]
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:coupon_lower_bound} {\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:thm:coupon_lower_bound}{$\downarrow$}}]}
There exists a universal constant \(c>0\) such that, for every
\(p,q\in[1,\infty]\) and \(T\ge3\), and every dimension \(d\ge T\), we have
\[
 
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:worst-case-convex-hull-distance}{\normalcolor \operatorname{ConvDist}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\mathrm{wc}] 
[p,q^\ast,T]
    \ge c\frac{\log T}{T}.
\]
The lower bound is witnessed by a mean-zero distribution supported on
\(B_{q^\ast}^d\).
<!-- end proposition-2 -->

<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:first-order-optimization-sfat}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:cor:first-order-optimization-sfat}{$\downarrow$}}]}
    Let \(1\le p \le q\le\infty\), \(d,T\in\mathbb N\), and ${\cal F}$ be the class of convex $1$-Lipschitz functions $\mathbb{R}^d \to \mathbb{R}$ in $\|\cdot\|_q$. There is a deterministic algorithm that for any $f\in {\cal F}$, after making $T$ queries to a first-order oracle, 
 
computes $\widehat{x}_T$ such that
\[
    f(\widehat x_T)-\min_{u\in B_p^d}f(u) = {\protect\hyperlink{def:big_O_p}{\normalcolor \widetilde{O}_{p,q}}}\left( 
        \frac{1}{
            T^{\frac1p-(\frac1q-\frac12)_+}
        }
     \right).
\]
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:explicit_regimes} {\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:cor:explicit_regimes}{$\downarrow$}}]}
    Assume \(0<\alpha \le 2 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast] \). Then,
\( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])\) lies, up to constants depending only on
\(p,q\), between the following quantity and \(L_\alpha\) times it:
\[
\begin{cases}
\displaystyle
\min\left\{
    \alpha^{-\left(\frac{1}{p}-(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2})_+\right)^{-1}},\;
    d\left(
        1+\log_+
        \frac{1}{\alpha d^{\frac{1}{p}-(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2})_+}}
    \right)
\right\},
& p<q,\\[4mm]
\displaystyle
\min\left\{
    \left(\frac{d^{1/q-1/p}}{\alpha}\right)^{\max\{q,2\}},\;
    d\left(
        1+\log_+
        \frac{d^{1/q-1/p}}{\alpha d^{1/\max\{q,2\}}}
    \right)
\right\},
& p\ge q.
\end{cases}
\]
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:sobolev-function-space-example}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:prop:sobolev-function-space-example}{$\downarrow$}}]}
For every $m\ge1$ and $0<s\le m/2$, for all sufficiently small $\alpha>0$,
\begin{equation*}
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]
    \left(
         
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]
    \right)
    \asymp_{s,m}
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]
    \left(
         
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]
    \right)
    \asymp_{s,m}
    \alpha^{-\frac{2m}{m+2s}}.
\end{equation*}
Consequently,
\begin{equation*}
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:minimax-optimization-error}{\normalcolor \mathcal E\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
](T)
    =
    \widetilde\Theta_{s,m}
    \left(
        T^{-\left(\frac12+\frac{s}{m}\right)}
    \right).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end proposition-3 -->

[#1][#2]

Consequently,

[#1]

<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:basis_bound}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:prop:basis_bound}{$\downarrow$}}]}
For all sufficiently small $\alpha>0$,
\begin{equation*}

[#1][#2]

[#1][#2]

,

])
    \asymp_p
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[

,

])
    \asymp_p
    \alpha^{-p}.
\end{equation*}
Consequently, after symmetrizing the coefficient functionals,
\begin{equation*}
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:minimax-optimization-error}{\normalcolor \mathcal E\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1] 
[

,

<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{thm:generalized_triplex_like_theorem}. }

F or each realized past, write \(\mathbb E_t'\) for the conditional expectation
over independent draws \(x_t'\sim s_t\) and \(\ell_t'\sim r_t\):
\[
    \mathbb E_t' F(x_t',\ell_t')
    :=
    \mathbb E_{\substack{x_t'\sim s_t\\\ell_t'\sim r_t}}
    \left[F(x_t',\ell_t')\mid x_{1:t-1},\ell_{1:t-1}\right].
\]
These draws are used only inside the conditional expectation; future
distributions depend on the realized, unprimed history.

Using \cref{ass:minimax_theorem_consequence} gives \( 
    \IfNoValueTF{\)}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};}}{\)} } 
.
Adding and subtracting the conditional expectations and splitting
\(\sup_u\) gives \( 
    \IfNoValueTF{\)}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {2};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {2};}}{\)} } 
:
{\small
\begin{align*}
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T]
    &\circled{1}[=]
    \left(\sup_{r_t}\inf_{s_t}
    \mathbb E_{\substack{x_t\sim s_t\\\ell_t\sim r_t}}\right)_{t=1}^T
    \sup_{u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}
    \left\{\sum_{t=1}^T\ell_t(x_t)-T\max_{t\in[T]}\ell_t(u)\right\}\\
    &\circled{2}[\le]
    \left(\sup_{r_t}\inf_{s_t}
    \mathbb E_{\substack{x_t\sim s_t\\\ell_t\sim r_t}}\right)_{t=1}^T
    \Bigg[
        \sum_{t=1}^T\left(\ell_t(x_t)-\mathbb E_t'\ell_t'(x_t')\right)
    +
        \sup_{u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}\left\{
            \sum_{t=1}^T\mathbb E_t'
            \left(\ell_t'(x_t')-\ell_t'(u)\right)
        \right\}\\
    &\qquad+
        \sup_{u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}\left\{
            \sum_{t=1}^T\mathbb E_t'\ell_t'(u)
            -T\max_{t\in[T]}\ell_t(u)
        \right\}
    \Bigg].
\end{align*}
}

We break this expression into three terms in the spirit of the Triplex
Inequality \citep[Theorem~1]{rakhlin2011online}, allowing different loss
aggregations for the player and comparator. Using linearity of expectation
and, at each infimum,
\[
    \inf_a[C_1(a)+C_2(a)+C_3(a)]
    \le
    \sup_a C_1(a)+\inf_a C_2(a)+\sup_a C_3(a),
\]
we obtain
{\small
\begin{align*}
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T]
    &\le
    \left(\sup_{r_t}\sup_{s_t}
    \mathbb E_{\substack{x_t\sim s_t\\\ell_t\sim r_t}}\right)_{t=1}^T
    \left\{\sum_{t=1}^T
        \left(\ell_t(x_t)-\mathbb E_t'\ell_t'(x_t')\right)
    \right\}\\
    &\quad+
    \left(\sup_{r_t}\inf_{s_t}
    \mathbb E_{\substack{x_t\sim s_t\\\ell_t\sim r_t}}\right)_{t=1}^T
    \sup_{u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}
    \left\{\sum_{t=1}^T\mathbb E_t'
        \left(\ell_t'(x_t')-\ell_t'(u)\right)
    \right\}\\
    &\quad+
    \left(\sup_{r_t}\sup_{s_t}
    \mathbb E_{\substack{x_t\sim s_t\\\ell_t\sim r_t}}\right)_{t=1}^T
    \sup_{u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}
    \left\{\sum_{t=1}^T\mathbb E_t'\ell_t'(u)
        -T\max_{t\in[T]}\ell_t(u)
    \right\}.
\end{align*}
}
The first term is zero by the tower property, since
\(\mathbb E_t'\ell_t'(x_t')
=\mathbb E[\ell_t(x_t)\mid x_{1:t-1},\ell_{1:t-1}]\).
For the second term, fix \(\eta>0\). Since \(r_t\) is announced before
\(s_t\) in the minimax-swapped game, choose \(s_t\) as the pure strategy of
playing a measurable \(\eta\)-optimal point of
\[
    \inf_{x\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}
    \mathbb E_{\ell_t'\sim r_t(\cdot\mid x_{1:t-1},\ell_{1:t-1})}
    \ell_t'(x).
\]
Then \(\mathbb E_t'(\ell_t'(x_t')-\ell_t'(u))\le\eta\) for every \(u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\)
along every realized path, so the second term is at most \(T\eta\).


In the third term, the payoff depends only on the chosen loss kernels and
sampled losses. Player actions only provide auxiliary randomness for choosing
future kernels. By backward induction, averaging over these actions cannot
exceed the best continuation, so their distributions can be replaced by
deterministic choices. For any fixed resulting strategy, those choices can
be reconstructed from the loss history. Hence the kernels may be written
as \(r_t(\cdot\mid\ell_{1:t-1})\).  Finally, substituting the notation
\(
    \mathbb E_t'\ell_t'(u)
    =
    \int\ell(u)\,r_t(d\ell\mid\ell_{1:t-1})
    =m_t(u)
\)
and letting \(\eta\downarrow0\) proves the claim.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:lower-bound-sfat}
{
Under \cref{ass:minimax_theorem_consequence}, for every \(\alpha>0\),
\[
    \frac{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T]}{T}
    \ge
    \frac{\alpha}{2}
    \left(
        \frac{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])}{T}
        \wedge 1
    \right).
\]
}
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Fix \(\alpha>0\) and set
\[
    m:=\min\{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]),T\}.
\]
If \(m=0\), the right-hand side is zero, and the identically zero adversarial
strategy shows that \( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T]\ge0\). Hence, suppose \(m\ge1\).
Fix a depth-\(m\) predictable tree \((g_t)_{t=1}^m\) over \( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\) and a
threshold tree \((s_t)_{t=1}^m\) that are sequentially \(\alpha\)-shattered
by \( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]\). This class is bounded in
\([- 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
], 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]]\). Since both children of every
shattered node are realizable,
\[
    - 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]+\frac{\alpha}{2}
    \le s_t
    \le 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]-\frac{\alpha}{2}.
\]
In particular, \(\alpha/2\le 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]\).

Let \(\varepsilon_1,\ldots,\varepsilon_m\) be iid Rademacher signs and set
\[
    (G_t,B_t)
    :=
    \begin{cases}
        \bigl(
            -\varepsilon_tg_t(\varepsilon_{<t}),
            \varepsilon_ts_t(\varepsilon_{<t})
        \bigr),
        &t\le m,\\
        (0,-\alpha/2),
        &m<t\le T,
    \end{cases}
    \qquad
    Y_t=(G_t,B_t).
\]
The symmetry assumption \( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
=- 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\) ensures that \(G_t\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\) for
\(t\le m\), and convexity and symmetry give \(0\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\) on the padded rounds.
The bounds above give
\(B_t\in[- 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
], 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]]\) in both cases. Hence
\(Y_t\in 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:affine-sample-space}{\normalcolor \mathcal Z\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]\), so this is an admissible adaptive adversarial
strategy.

For \(t\le m\), the sign \(\varepsilon_t\) is independent of the past and has
mean zero; for \(t>m\), \(Y_t\) is deterministic. Therefore
\[
    \mu_t
    =
    \mathbb E[G_t\mid Y_{1:t-1}]
    =0,
    \qquad
    \beta_t
    =
    \mathbb E[B_t\mid Y_{1:t-1}]
    =
    \begin{cases}
        0,&t\le m,\\
        -\alpha/2,&m<t\le T.
    \end{cases}
\]

Using the witnesses \(u_\varepsilon\) from
\cref{def:fat-shattering-dimensions}, for \(t\le m\) we have
\[
    -\langle G_t,u_\varepsilon\rangle-B_t
    =
    \varepsilon_t
    \left(
        \langle g_t(\varepsilon_{<t}),u_\varepsilon\rangle
        -
        s_t(\varepsilon_{<t})
    \right)
    \ge
    \frac{\alpha}{2}.
\]
For \(t>m\), the left-hand side equals \(\alpha/2\). Moreover, against this
fixed adversarial strategy, every player strategy has expected cumulative loss
\(\sum_{t=1}^T\beta_t=-(T-m)\alpha/2\). Therefore, after using \cref{ass:minimax_theorem_consequence}, we lower bounding the minimax value by the one for our specific adversarial strategy and obtain:
 
\[
\begin{aligned}
    \frac{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T]}{T}
    &\ge
    \mathbb E\sup_{u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}
    \left\{
        -\frac{(T-m)\alpha}{2T}
        +
        \min_{t\in[T]}
        \bigl(-\langle G_t,u\rangle-B_t\bigr)
    \right\}\\
    &\ge
    -\frac{(T-m)\alpha}{2T}
    +
    \mathbb E
    \min_{t\in[T]}
    \bigl(-\langle G_t,u_\varepsilon\rangle-B_t\bigr)\\
    &\ge
    -\frac{(T-m)\alpha}{2T}
    +
    \frac{\alpha}{2}
    =
    \frac{m\alpha}{2T}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Substituting the definition of \(m\) proves the claim.
<!-- end proof-3 -->

\textbf{of \cref{thm:fully-sequential-bound}. }

The lower bound is \cref{prop:lower-bound-sfat}. For the upper bound, write $\Lambda:= 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:fully-sequential-bound][#2] 
[

,

]$ and $\widetilde 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 

    :=
    \Lambda^{-1} 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$.
Scaling every affine loss by $1/\Lambda$ gives
\begin{equation*}
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:fully-sequential-bound][\max] 
[

,

,T]
    =
    \Lambda
      
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:fully-sequential-bound][\max] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,\widetilde 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T],
    \qquad
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:fully-sequential-bound][#2] 
[\alpha]
    \left(
         
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:fully-sequential-bound][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,\widetilde 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]
    \right)
    =
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:fully-sequential-bound][#2] 
[\Lambda\alpha]
    \left(
         
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:fully-sequential-bound][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]
    \right).
\end{equation*}
It therefore suffices to prove the normalized upper bound when $\Lambda=1$, which we assume below.
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
In this normalization, recall the body-indexed version of the offset
envelope class from \cref{def:offset-envelope-class}:
\[
    \Phi\circ(\tfrac12 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:affine-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal A\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:fully-sequential-bound][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])
    :=
    \left\{
        (g,b)\mapsto
        \phi\!\left(\frac{\langle g,u\rangle+b}{2}\right):
        u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,\ \phi\in\Phi
    \right\}.
\]
Fix $0<\alpha\le1$. We obtain, for
every adaptive adversarial strategy \(\pi\):
\begin{align*}
\begin{aligned}
    \mathbb{E}_{\pi}[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:normalized-approximation-functional}{\normalcolor \mathfrak{G}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:fully-sequential-bound][ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T](Y_{1:T})]
    &\circled{1}[\le] 
    2 \, \mathbb{E} _\pi \sup_{f\in\Phi\circ(\tfrac12 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:affine-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal A\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:fully-sequential-bound][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])}
    \left[
        \frac1T\sum_{t=1}^T P_tf - \frac2T\sum_{t=1}^T f(Y_t)
    \right] \\
    &\circled{2}[\le]
    C\alpha + \frac{C}{T} \left[ 1+ \int_\alpha^1 \log {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-covering-number}{\normalcolor N_\infty^{\mathrm{seq}}}}(c_{\mathrm{off}}\beta,\Phi\circ(\tfrac12

, we used \(\int_\alpha^1\beta^{-1}\,d\beta\le\log(e/\alpha)\), the bound of sequential covering numbers by $

[c\beta]$ from
\citep[Corollary~1]{rakhlin2015online}, and we renamed \(c_{\mathrm{off}}/4\) as the universal scale constant \(c\).
The deterministic-domination and affine-entropy proofs use only the normalized

,

The right-hand side does not depend on the adversarial strategy \(\pi\). Hence,
for every \(\varepsilon>0\), choosing an \(\varepsilon\)-optimal
history-dependent adversarial strategy
\(\pi^\varepsilon\) for the nested problem yields
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
\begin{equation*}
    \frac{

[proof:thm:fully-sequential-bound][\max] 
[

,

,T]}{T}
    \le
    \mathbb E_{\pi^\varepsilon}
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:normalized-approximation-functional}{\normalcolor \mathfrak{G}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:fully-sequential-bound][ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,

,T](Y_{1:T})
    +
    \varepsilon.
\end{equation*}
Letting \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\) and then taking the infimum over
\(0<\alpha\le1\) proves the normalized upper bound. Multiplying it by
\(\Lambda\), substituting \(\alpha'=\Lambda\alpha\) and
\(\beta'=\Lambda\beta\), and then dropping the primes gives the stated upper
bound for \(( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
)\).


 

\textbf{High-dimensional instantiation in the $(p, q)$ case}. When \(T\le d\), the endpoint
\(p=q=\infty\) follows by choosing \(\alpha=1\) in the $\inf$ of the upper bound, combining the
 
endpoint case of \cref{cor:explicit_regimes} with \cref{prop:lower-bound-sfat}. For proving the remaining regimes, we study the general case where $ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]$ decays like $\alpha^{-r}$ for $r \geq 1$.

In particular, for the remaining regimes with \(p\le q\), write
\( 
    r
    :=
    \left(
        \frac{1}{p}
        -
        \left(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}\right)_+
    \right)^{-1},
\)
so that
\(
    T^{-1/r}
    =
    T^{-(1/p-(1/q-1/2)_+)}.
\)

The class \( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d]\) is exactly the linear class whose sequential
fat-shattering dimension is characterized in \cref{cor:explicit_regimes}.
The upper side of that result gives
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\beta]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \le
    C_{p,q}
    \beta^{-r}
    \left(1+\log_+\frac{ed}{\beta}\right),
    \qquad 0<\beta\le1.
\]
Substituting this estimate into the upper bound proved above gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
    \frac{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[p,q^\ast,T]}{T}
    \le
    \inf_{0<\alpha\le1}
    \Bigg\{
        C\alpha
        &+
        \frac{C}{T}\log\!\frac{e}{\alpha}
        +
        \frac{C}{T}
        +
        \frac{C_{p,q}}{T}
        \int_\alpha^1
        \beta^{-r}
        \left(1+\log_+\frac{ed}{\beta}\right)
        \log^\gamma\!\left(\frac{eT}{\beta}\right)
        \,d\beta
    \Bigg\}.
\end{aligned}
\]

First suppose \(r=1\). Choose \(\alpha=T^{-1}\). Then
\[
    1+\log_+\frac{ed}{\beta}\le C\log(edT),
    \qquad
    \log\!\left(\frac{eT}{\beta}\right)\le C\log(eT)
    \qquad(T^{-1}\le\beta\le1),
\]
and therefore for a constant $C_{p, q, \gamma}$:
\[
\begin{aligned}
    \frac{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[p,q^\ast,T]}{T}
    &\le
    \frac{C}{T}
    +
    \frac{C}{T}\log(eT)
    +
    \frac{C_{p,q,\gamma}}{T}
    \log(edT)
    \int_{T^{-1}}^1
    \frac{1}{\beta}
    \log^\gamma\!\left(\frac{eT}{\beta}\right)
    \,d\beta
    \\
    &\le
    \frac{C}{T}
    +
    \frac{C}{T}\log(eT)
    +
    \frac{C_{p,q,\gamma}}{T}
    \log(edT)
    \log^{\gamma+1}(eT) = {\protect\hyperlink{def:big_O_tilde}{\normalcolor \widetilde{O}}}\left( \frac{1}{T^{1 / r}} \right).
\end{aligned}
\]
This is the asserted bound when \(r=1\).
Now suppose \(r>1\). If \(T=1\), the claim is trivial after increasing the
constant, so assume \(T\ge2\).  For every \(\alpha\ge T^{-1}\),
\[
    \int_\alpha^1
    \beta^{-r}
    \left(1+\log_+\frac{ed}{\beta}\right)
    \log^\gamma\!\left(\frac{eT}{\beta}\right)
    \,d\beta
    \le
    C_{r,\gamma}
    \alpha^{1-r}
    \log(edT)
    \log^\gamma(eT/\alpha).
\]
Thus
\[
\begin{aligned}
    \frac{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[p,q^\ast,T]}{T}
    &\le
    \inf_{T^{-1}<\alpha\le1}
    \left\{
        C\alpha
        +
        \frac{C}{T}\log\!\frac{e}{\alpha}
        +
        \frac{C}{T}
        +
        \frac{C_{p,q,r,\gamma}}{T}
        \alpha^{1-r}
        \log(edT)
        \log^\gamma(eT/\alpha)
    \right\} \\
    &\circled{1}[\leq]
    C_{p,q,r,\gamma}
    \left(
        \frac{
            \log(edT)\log^\gamma(eT)
        }{T}
    \right)^{1/r}
    +
    \frac{C}{T}\log(eT) \\
    &= {\protect\hyperlink{def:big_O_tilde}{\normalcolor \widetilde{O}}}\left( \frac{1}{T^{1 / r}} \right).
\end{aligned}
\]
where $ 
    \IfNoValueTF{$}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};}}{$} } 
 uses
\(
    \alpha
    =
    \min\left\{
        1,\,
        \left(
            \frac{
                \log(edT)\log^\gamma(eT)
            }{T}
        \right)^{1/r}
    \right\} > \frac{1}{T},
\)
since if the minimum is attained at \(1\), the displayed claim is trivial after
increasing the constant. Otherwise, this choice satisfies \(\log(eT/\alpha)\le C_r\log(eT)\).

For the lower bound, it is enough that the first term in
\cref{cor:explicit_regimes} be active, up to constants, at some scale
\(\alpha\asymp T^{-1/r}\); thus \(T\le d\) is sufficient but not necessary.
Indeed, set \(\alpha_T:= T^{-1/r}\). At this scale, the first term in that
corollary equals \(T\), while its saturated term equals \(d\) when \(T\le d\),
because \(\alpha_Td^{1/r}=(d/T)^{1/r}\ge1\). Hence, for some
\(c_{p,q}>0\),
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha_T]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \ge c_{p,q}T.
\]
By \cref{prop:lower-bound-sfat},
\[
    \frac{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:max-regret-value}{\normalcolor V\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\max] 
[p,q^\ast,T]}{T}
    \ge
    \frac{\alpha_T}{2}
    \left(
        \frac{
             
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha_T]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
        }{T}
        \wedge 1
    \right)
    \ge
    \frac{c_{p,q}\wedge1}{2T^{1/r}},
\]
which is the stated lower bound after renaming the constant.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:one-dimensional-general}
With the notation above and \(\Phi\) as in
\cref{def:offset-envelope-class},
\[
    \bar m
    -
    \max_{\lambda\in\Delta_T^{(2)}}
    \sum_{t=1}^T\lambda_t z_t
    \le
    \sup_{\phi\in\Phi}
    \left[
        \frac1T\sum_{t=1}^T\int \phi\,dQ_t
        -
        \frac2T\sum_{t=1}^T\phi(z_t)
    \right].
\]
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Let \(\nu:= 2T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^T\delta_{z_t}\). The measure $\nu$ has total mass
$2$.  Maximizing \(\sum_{t=1}^T\lambda_t z_t\) over
\(\lambda\in\Delta_T^{(2)}\) is equivalent to maximizing
$\int z\,d\theta(z)$ over all submeasures $\theta\le\nu$ with total mass $1$,
where $\theta$ being a submeasure of $\nu$ means that $\theta(A)\le\nu(A)$ for every measurable set
$A$.
The maximizing submeasure fills mass from the largest observed points downward.
If $G$ denotes its distribution function, then
\[
    G(a)=\bigl(\widehat F(a)-1\bigr)_+.
\]
Let \(\bar Q:= T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^T Q_t\). Then $\bar Q$ has distribution
function $\bar F$ and mean $\bar m$.  For any probability measure $\gamma$ on
$[-1,1]$ with distribution function $G_\gamma$, we have
\(
    \int z\,d\gamma(z)
    =
    1-\int_{-1}^1G_\gamma(a)\,da.
\)
Therefore
\[
\begin{aligned}
    \bar m
    -
    \max_{\lambda\in\Delta_T^{(2)}}
    \sum_{t=1}^T\lambda_t z_t
    &= \bar m-\int z\,d\theta(z)
    =
    \left[1-\int_{-1}^1\bar F(a)\,da\right]
    -
    \left[1-\int_{-1}^1G(a)\,da\right] \\
    &= \int_{-1}^1\bigl(G(a)-\bar F(a)\bigr)\,da
    \le
    \int_{-1}^1
    \bigl(
        \widehat F(a)-\bar F(a)-1
    \bigr)_+,
\end{aligned}
\]
where the inequality follows from
\(G(a)=(\widehat F(a)-1)_+\) and \(\bar F(a)\ge0\). It remains to
identify the last integral.
Every $\phi\in\Phi$ admits a representation
\(
    \phi(z)=\int_{-1}^z h(a)\,da
\)
for some measurable $h:[-1,1]\to[0,1]$.  Conversely, every such $h$ defines a
function in $\Phi$.  Hence
\[
    \int \phi\,dQ_t
    =
    \int_{-1}^1 h(a)\bigl(1-F_t(a)\bigr)\,da,
    \qquad
    \text{and}
    \qquad
    \phi(z_t)
    =
    \int_{-1}^1 h(a)\mathbf{1}_{\{a\le z_t\}}\,da.
\]
Therefore
\[
    \frac1T\sum_{t=1}^T\int \phi\,dQ_t
    -
    \frac2T\sum_{t=1}^T\phi(z_t)
    =
    \int_{-1}^1
        h(a)
        \bigl(
            \widehat F(a)-\bar F(a)-1
        \bigr)
    \,da.
\]
Taking the supremum over measurable $h:[-1,1]\to[0,1]$ gives the positive part
integral, and therefore the claim.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:deterministic-domination-general}
Suppose that $ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]=1$. For every realized path,
\begin{equation*}
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:normalized-approximation-functional}{\normalcolor \mathfrak{G}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T](y_{1:T})
    \le
    2
    \sup_{f\in\Phi\circ(\tfrac12 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:affine-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal A\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])}
    \left[
        \frac1T
        \sum_{t=1}^T
        P_tf
        -
        \frac2T
        \sum_{t=1}^T
        f(y_t)
    \right].
\end{equation*}
Above, the function  class in the supremum is defined in \cref{def:offset-envelope-class}.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Fix $u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$ and define $z_t^u
    :=
    \langle g_t,u\rangle+b_t$. 
Its predictable mean is
\begin{equation*}
    m_t^u
    :=
    \int_{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:affine-sample-space}{\normalcolor \mathcal Z\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]}
    \left(
        \langle g,u\rangle+b
    \right)
    \,dP_t(g,b\mid y_{1:t-1})
    =
    \langle \mu_t,u\rangle+\beta_t,
\end{equation*}
where we abbreviate $\mu_t=\mu_t(y_{1:t-1})$ and $\beta_t=\beta_t(y_{1:t-1})$. Since $ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]=1$, we have $|\langle g_t,u\rangle|\le1$. Together with $b_t\in[-1,1]$, this gives $z_t^u\in[-2,2]$. Let $Q_t^u$ be the pushforward of $P_t(\cdot\mid y_{1:t-1})$ under
\begin{equation*}
    (g,b)
    \mapsto
    \frac{\langle g,u\rangle+b}{2}.
\end{equation*}
Then $Q_t^u$ is supported on $[-1,1]$ and has mean $m_t^u/2$. Therefore,
\begin{align*}
    \frac1T\sum_{t=1}^T m_t^u
    -
    \max_{\lambda\in\Delta_T}
    \sum_{t=1}^T\lambda_t z_t^u
    &\le
    \frac1T\sum_{t=1}^T m_t^u
    -
    \max_{\lambda\in\Delta_T^{(2)}}
    \sum_{t=1}^T\lambda_t z_t^u \\
    &=
    2
    \left[
        \frac1T\sum_{t=1}^T\frac{m_t^u}{2}
        -
        \max_{\lambda\in\Delta_T^{(2)}}
        \sum_{t=1}^T\lambda_t\frac{z_t^u}{2}
    \right] \\
    &\circled{1}[\le]
    2
    \sup_{\phi\in\Phi}
    \left[
        \frac1T\sum_{t=1}^T
        P_t
        \phi\left(
            \frac{\langle \cdot,u\rangle+\cdot}{2}
        \right)
        -
        \frac2T\sum_{t=1}^T
        \phi\left(
            \frac{\langle g_t,u\rangle+b_t}{2}
        \right)
    \right].
\end{align*}
Here $ 
    \IfNoValueTF{$}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};}}{$} } 
 applies \cref{lem:one-dimensional-general} to $Q_1^u,\ldots,Q_T^u$ and $z_1^u/2,\ldots,z_T^u/2$. Taking the supremum over $u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$ proves the claim.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:finite-class-offset}
Let \(b>0\), and let \(\mathcal A\) be a finite nonempty family of nonnegative
pair-trees
\(
    a
    =
    \left\{
        a_t^+(\varepsilon_{1:t-1}),
        a_t^-(\varepsilon_{1:t-1})
    \right\}_{t\le T}
\)
with values in \([0,b]\).  For
\begin{equation}
    M_a(\varepsilon)
    :=
    \sum_{t=1}^T
    \left[
        a_t^{-\varepsilon_t}(\varepsilon_{1:t-1})
        -
        2a_t^{\varepsilon_t}(\varepsilon_{1:t-1})
    \right] \quad \text{we have} \quad
    \mathbb E_\varepsilon
    \left[
        \sup_{a\in\mathcal A}
        \frac1T M_a(\varepsilon)
    \right]_+
    \le
    \frac{4b}{T}
    \bigl(1+\log|\mathcal A|\bigr).
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Indeed, fix \(a\in\mathcal A\).  Conditionally on \(\varepsilon_{1:t-1}\), write
\(
    r=\frac{a_t^+(\varepsilon_{1:t-1})}{b},
    s=\frac{a_t^-(\varepsilon_{1:t-1})}{b}.
\)
Then \(r,s\in[0,1]\).  For a sufficiently small universal \(\lambda>0\),
\[
    \frac12 e^{\lambda(s-2r)}
    +
    \frac12 e^{\lambda(r-2s)}
    \le 1.
\]
Indeed, for fixed \(s\), the left-hand side is convex in \(r\), so its maximum
over \(r\in[0,1]\) is attained at \(r=0\) or \(r=1\).  For each of these two
choices, the resulting function of \(s\) is again convex, so it is enough to
check the four corners of \([0,1]^2\).  The claim holds, for example, for
\(\lambda=1/4\).  Therefore
\(
    \mathbb E_\varepsilon \exp(\lambda M_a(\varepsilon)/b)\le 1
\).
Hence
\(
    \mathbb P_\varepsilon(M_a(\varepsilon)>u)\le e^{-\lambda u/b}
\).
By a union bound,
\[
    \mathbb P_\varepsilon
    \left(
        \sup_{a\in\mathcal A}M_a(\varepsilon)>u
    \right)
    \le
    \min\{1,|\mathcal A|e^{-\lambda u/b}\}.
\]
Writing \(N=|\mathcal A|\) and integrating at the cutoff
\(u_0=4b\log N\) gives
\[
    \mathbb E_\varepsilon\left[\sup_{a\in\mathcal A}M_a(\varepsilon)\right]_+
    \le
    u_0+\int_{u_0}^\infty Ne^{-u/(4b)}\,du
    =4b(1+\log N).
\]
Dividing by \(T\) proves the lemma.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
\label{prop:sequential-offset}
Let $\mathcal Z$ be any measurable space and let \(\mathcal C\) be a nonempty
pointwise-measurable class of measurable functions \(\mathcal Z\to[0,2]\).
For every adaptive strategy of
kernels $P_t(\cdot\mid X_{1:t-1})$ supported on $\mathcal Z$, and every
$0<\delta\le 1$,
\[
\begin{aligned}
    &\mathbb E
    \sup_{h\in\mathcal C}
    \left[
        \frac1T\sum_{t=1}^T P_th
        -
        \frac2T\sum_{t=1}^T h(X_t)
    \right]
    \le
    4\delta
    +
    \frac{16}{T}
    +
    \frac{48}{T}
        \int_\delta^1
        \log
        {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-covering-number}{\normalcolor N_\infty^{\mathrm{seq}}}}(\alpha/4,\mathcal C,2T)
        \,d\alpha,
\end{aligned}
\]
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
We use are going to use a sequential symmetrization technique, at the level of predictable trees. The inspiration is \citep{rakhlin2015martingale} that also consider symmetrization for different adaptive processes. At every node of the adaptive process, draw two independent children from the distribution chosen at that node, obtaining a random pair-tree
\[
    \mathbf Z
    =
    \left\{
        Z_t^+(\varepsilon_{1:t-1}),
        Z_t^-(\varepsilon_{1:t-1})
    \right\}_{t\le T}.
\]
For a sign path $\varepsilon\in\{\pm1\}^T$, the selected point at time $t$ is
\(Z_t^{\varepsilon_t}(\varepsilon_{1:t-1})\), and the tangent ghost point is
\(Z_t^{-\varepsilon_t}(\varepsilon_{1:t-1})\).
Let $\mathcal F_{t-1}$ be the sigma-field generated by the past signs and the sampled pairs along the selected path up to time $t-1$, and write $P_t$ for the kernel evaluated at the selected history. Then
\[
    P_th
    =\mathbb E[h(Z_t^{\varepsilon_t})\mid\mathcal F_{t-1}]
    =\mathbb E[h(Z_t^{-\varepsilon_t})\mid\mathcal F_{t-1}].
\]
Conditionally on the entire selected path and the signs, each ghost point still has law $P_t$, since future selected points depend only on the selected history, not on the ghosts. The selected path has the law of $X_{1:T}$, so conditional Jensen's inequality gives:
\[
    \mathbb E\sup_{h\in\mathcal C}\sum_{t=1}^T(P_th-2h(X_t))
    \le
    \mathbb E_{\mathbf Z,\varepsilon}\sup_{h\in\mathcal C}
    \sum_{t=1}^T
    \left[
        h(Z_t^{-\varepsilon_t}(\varepsilon_{1:t-1}))
        -
        2h(Z_t^{\varepsilon_t}(\varepsilon_{1:t-1}))
    \right].
\]
We average over the random sign and the two
terms always use different children of the same node. It remains to bound the right-hand side conditionally on $\mathbf Z$ for which we perform a chaining argument.

The left-hand side of the proposition is at most \(2\). Hence the result is
immediate when \(\delta\ge1/2\), and we assume below that \(0<\delta<1/2\).
Fix the pair-tree $\mathbf Z$ and fix
$0<\delta\le 1$.  Let
\begin{equation}\label{eq:choice_of_S}
    S:= \max\{s\ge 0:2^{-s}\ge\delta\},
    \qquad
    \alpha_s:= 2^{-s}.
\end{equation}
At scale $\alpha_s$, take a pair-tree sequential cover $\mathcal V_s$
of $\mathcal C$ at accuracy $\alpha_s/2$.  A pair-tree of depth $T$ can be
encoded as an ordinary tree of depth $2T$ by listing, at each time $t$, first
the ``$+$'' child and then the ``$-$'' child.  Therefore we may choose
$\mathcal V_s$ with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cover_size_bound}
    \log|\mathcal V_s|
    \le
    \log
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-covering-number}{\normalcolor N_\infty^{\mathrm{seq}}}}(\alpha_s/2,\mathcal C,2T).
\end{equation}
We also clip the cover trees to $[0,2]$, which does not increase the covering
error.

By \cref{def:sequential-cover}, the approximation guarantee is pathwise. Fix
$h\in\mathcal C$ and a sign path $\varepsilon$.  For each scale $s$, choose
$v_s\in\mathcal V_s$ approximating $h$ along the path on both children.  Define
the notation \(v_{s,t}^{+}\) for the cover value approximating
\(h(Z_t^+(\varepsilon_{<t}))\), that is, the plus child of $v_s$ at time $t$. Similarly \(v_{s,t}^{-}\) for
\(h(Z_t^-(\varepsilon_{<t}))\). We now bound both quantities at once using the
notation \(v_{s,t}^{\pm}\). By definition of the covers at scales $\alpha_r/2$, along the chosen path we have
the approximations
\begin{equation}\label{eq:max_lb_bounds}
   0 \leq  c_{s,t}^{\pm}
    := 
    \max_{0\le r\le s} \bigl(v_{r,t}^{\pm}-\alpha_r/2\bigr)_+ \leq h(Z_t^{\pm}) \leq c_{s, t}^{\pm} + \alpha_s \quad \text{ for all }s.
\end{equation}
Define increments by
\begin{equation} \label{eq:bounding_g_st}
    g_{0,t}^{\pm}:= c_{0,t}^{\pm} \in [0, 2], \ \ \text{ and, for } s\ge 1,
    \ \  g_{s,t}^{\pm}
    := 
    \min\left\{
        c_{s,t}^{\pm}-c_{s-1,t}^{\pm},
        \alpha_{s-1}
    \right\} \in [0, \alpha_{s-1}].
\end{equation}
The truncation is needed only off the chosen path: it gives every possible
increment tree the uniform envelope \(\alpha_{s-1}\) required by the
finite-class estimate in \cref{lem:finite-class-offset}. Along the chosen path it is harmless, since
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sum_of_increments}
    c_{s,t}^{\pm}-c_{s-1,t}^{\pm}
    \le
    h(Z_t^\pm)-c_{s-1,t}^{\pm}
    \le
    \alpha_{s-1} \quad \text{ and so } \quad
    c_{S,t}^{\pm}
    =
    \sum_{s=0}^S g_{s,t}^{\pm}.
\end{equation}
Using that by \cref{eq:choice_of_S} we have \(\delta\le\alpha_S<2\delta\) and using \cref{eq:max_lb_bounds}, we have
\[
    c_{S,t}^{\pm} \leq
    h(Z_t^\pm)
    \le
    c_{S,t}^{\pm}+2\delta,
\] which along with \cref{eq:sum_of_increments}, yields $ 
    \IfNoValueTF{$}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};}}{$} } 
 below:
\[
\begin{aligned}
    \mathbb E_\varepsilon
    \sup_{h\in\mathcal C}
    \frac1T
    \sum_{t=1}^T
    \left[
        h(Z_t^{-\varepsilon_t})
        -
        2h(Z_t^{\varepsilon_t})
    \right]
    &\circled{1}[\le]
    2\delta
    +
    \sum_{s=0}^{S}
    \mathbb E_\varepsilon
    \left[
        \sup_{g\in\mathcal G_s}
        \frac1T
        \sum_{t=1}^T
        \left[
            g_t^{-\varepsilon_t}(\varepsilon_{<t})
            -
            2g_t^{\varepsilon_t}(\varepsilon_{<t})
        \right]
    \right]_+ \\
    &\circled{2}[\leq]
    2\delta
    +
    \frac{8}{T}
    \sum_{s=0}^{S}
        \alpha_s
        \left(
            1+
            \sum_{r=0}^s \log|\mathcal V_r|
        \right)
    \\
    &\circled{3}[\le]
    2\delta
    +
    \frac{16}{T}
    \left[
        1+
        \sum_{s=0}^{S}
        \alpha_s\log|\mathcal V_s|
    \right] \\
    &\circled{4}[\le]
    2\delta
    +
    \frac{16}{T}
    +
    \frac{48}{T}
        \int_\delta^1
        \log {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-covering-number}{\normalcolor N_\infty^{\mathrm{seq}}}}(\alpha/4,\mathcal C,2T)\,d\alpha .
\end{aligned}
\]
where in $ 
    \IfNoValueTF{$}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {2};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {2};}}{$} } 
, we used the envelopes in \cref{eq:bounding_g_st} and
\cref{lem:finite-class-offset}. For \(s=0\), the envelope \(2\) gives the
factor \(8/T=8\alpha_0/T\). For \(s\ge1\), the envelope
\(\alpha_{s-1}=2\alpha_s\) gives \(8\alpha_s/T\). The possible increment
pair-trees at level $s$
form a finite class $\mathcal G_s$ satisfying
\(
    \log|\mathcal G_s|
    \le
    \sum_{r=0}^s\log|\mathcal V_r|
\). In $ 
    \IfNoValueTF{$}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {3};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {3};}}{$} } 
 we used that \(\sum_{s=0}^S\alpha_s \leq 2\) and that with  \(A_r=\log|\mathcal V_r|\), we have
\[
    \sum_{s=0}^{S}
    \alpha_s
    \sum_{r=0}^s A_r
    =
    \sum_{r=0}^{S}
    A_r
    \sum_{s=r}^{S}\alpha_s
    \le
    \sum_{r=0}^{S}
    A_r
    \sum_{s=r}^{\infty}2^{-s}
    =
    2\sum_{r=0}^{S}\alpha_r A_r .
\]
Finally, in $ 
    \IfNoValueTF{$}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {4};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {4};}}{$} } 
 we used the cover-size bound
\cref{eq:cover_size_bound} and monotonicity of the covering numbers. Indeed,
the term \(s=0\) is at most twice the integral over \([1/2,1]\) with covering
scale \(\alpha/4\), while each term \(s\ge1\) is at most the corresponding
integral over \([\alpha_s,\alpha_{s-1}]\). Since \(S\ge1\) and
\(\delta\le\alpha_S\), their sum is at most three times the integral over
\([\delta,1]\).
Averaging over \(\mathbf Z\) proves the proposition.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{cor:first-order-optimization-sfat}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:cor:first-order-optimization-sfat}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        \llap{\smash{ 
        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
            \setlength\@tempdima\HyperRaiseLinkDefault
            \HyperRaiseLinkHook
        \expandafter\endgroup
        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{ 
            \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
            \hypertarget{proof:cor:first-order-optimization-sfat}{} 
            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
F inite-dimensional compact balls satisfy
\cref{ass:minimax_theorem_consequence}. Apply
\cref{lemma:m-online-to-batch} to a minimax strategy for the \(\max\)-regret
game and output its average query. The rate is a consequence of
\cref{thm:fully-sequential-bound}.

We do not seek a computationally efficient strategy in this paper, but a valid
deterministic strategy can be obtained by taking sufficiently fine
discretizations of the iterated decision spaces \(B_p^d\) and
\(B_{q^\ast}^d\), using the Lipschitz continuity of \(\max\)-regret, and solving
    the resulting discrete problem coming from the deterministic definition of $V^{\max}_{p, q}$ by enumeration. This establishes first-order oracle efficiency, but the resulting computational time is exponential in \(d\) and \(T\).
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
\label{def:offset-envelope-class}
Define
\begin{equation*}
    \Phi
    :=
    \left\{
        \phi:[-1,1]\to[0,2]:
        \phi(-1)=0,\;
        \phi\ge0,\;
        \phi\text{ is nondecreasing and }1\text{-Lipschitz}
    \right\}.
\end{equation*}
The affine-composed class appearing in our analysis is defined as:
\begin{equation*}
    \Phi\circ(\tfrac12 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:affine-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal A\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])
    :=
    \left\{
        (g,b)
        \mapsto
        \phi
        \left(
            \frac{\langle g,u\rangle+b}{2}
        \right):
        u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,\;
        \phi\in\Phi
    \right\}.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:affine-offset-free-entropy}
With \(\Phi\) and \(\Phi\circ(\tfrac12 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:affine-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal A\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])\) as in
\cref{def:offset-envelope-class}, for every $n\ge1$ and every $0<\alpha\le1$,
\begin{equation*}
    \log
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-covering-number}{\normalcolor N_\infty^{\mathrm{seq}}}}
    \left(
        \alpha,
        \Phi\circ(\tfrac12 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:affine-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal A\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]),
        n
    \right)
    \le
    \log
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-covering-number}{\normalcolor N_\infty^{\mathrm{seq}}}}
    \left(
        \alpha/4,
         
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
],
        n
    \right)
    +
    \frac{17\log5}{\alpha}.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Fix a depth-$n$ tree
\begin{equation*}
    \mathbf z=(\mathbf g,\mathbf b),
    \qquad
    z_t(\varepsilon_{<t})
    =
    \left(
        g_t(\varepsilon_{<t}),
        b_t(\varepsilon_{<t})
    \right)
    \in
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\times[-1,1].
\end{equation*}
Let $\mathcal V$ be a sequential $\alpha/4$-cover of the linear class $ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]$ on the $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$-valued tree $\mathbf g$. Clip every value of every tree in $\mathcal V$ to $[-1,1]$; since the true linear evaluations lie in $[-1,1]$, this does not increase the covering error.




 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 



We also use a uniform $\alpha/4$-cover $\mathcal U$ of $\Phi$ in
\(\ell_\infty([-1,1])\), by real-valued functions on \([-1,1]\).  The
construction is elementary.  Put
\(\eta=\alpha/8\), choose a grid
\[
    -1=t_0<t_1<\cdots<t_M=1,
    \qquad
    t_j-t_{j-1}\le \eta,
    \qquad
    M\le \left\lceil\frac{2}{\eta}\right\rceil\le \frac{17}{\alpha}.
\]
For a given \(\phi\in\Phi\), quantize each \(\phi(t_j)\) to a nearest point
\(a_j\in\eta\mathbb Z\), set \(\psi(-1)=a_0\), and let \(\psi\) be
constant equal to \(a_j\) on \((t_{j-1},t_j]\) for \(1\le j\le M\). Then
\(\|\phi-\psi\|_\infty\le \eta/2+\eta\le\alpha/4\).  Moreover \(a_0=0\),
because \(\phi(-1)=0\), and the \(1\)-Lipschitz property gives
\[
    |a_j-a_{j-1}|
    \le
    |\phi(t_j)-\phi(t_{j-1})|+\eta
    \le 2\eta .
\]
Thus, after \(a_{j-1}\) is fixed, \(a_j\) has at most five possible
successors, namely the admissible grid values in
\(a_{j-1}+\{-2\eta,-\eta,0,\eta,2\eta\}\). Since \(a_0=0\) is fixed, only
\(a_1,\ldots,a_M\) contribute choices. Hence one may choose \(\mathcal U\) with
\[
    \log|\mathcal U|\le M\log 5\le \frac{17\log 5}{\alpha}.
\]
For each $v\in\mathcal V$ and $\psi\in\mathcal U$, define a real-valued tree on
$\mathbf z$ by
\[
    w_t^{v,\psi}(\varepsilon_{<t})
    := 
    \psi\!\left(
        \frac{
            v_t(\varepsilon_{<t})
            +
            b_t(\varepsilon_{<t})
        }{2}
    \right).
\]
The collection of all such trees has cardinality at most
$|\mathcal V||\mathcal U|$. Since $v_t\in[-1,1]$ and $b_t\in[-1,1]$, the argument of $\psi$ belongs to $[-1,1]$.

Now fix $u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$, $\phi\in\Phi$, and the corresponding function
\begin{equation*}
    f_{u,\phi}(g,b)
    :=
    \phi
    \left(
        \frac{\langle g,u\rangle+b}{2}
    \right)
    \in
    \Phi\circ(\tfrac12 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:affine-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal A\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]).
\end{equation*}
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Fix a root-to-leaf path $\varepsilon$.  Since \(\mathcal V\) covers the linear
class, choose a tree \(v\in\mathcal V\)which approximates the linear function
$g\mapsto\langle g,u\rangle$ along this path:
\begin{equation*}
    \max_{t\le n}
    \left|
        \langle g_t(\varepsilon_{<t}),u\rangle
        -
        v_t(\varepsilon_{<t})
    \right|
    \le
    \alpha/4.
\end{equation*}
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Choose \(\psi\in\mathcal U\) with
\(\|\phi-\psi\|_\infty\le \alpha/4\). Along this path, abbreviating
\(g_t=g_t(\varepsilon_{<t})\), \(b_t=b_t(\varepsilon_{<t})\), and
\(v_t=v_t(\varepsilon_{<t})\),
\begin{align*}
    \left|
        \phi
        \left(
            \frac{\langle g_t,u\rangle+b_t}{2}
        \right)
        -
        \psi
        \left(
            \frac{v_t+b_t}{2}
        \right)
    \right|
    &\le
    \left|
        \phi
        \left(
            \frac{\langle g_t,u\rangle+b_t}{2}
        \right)
        -
        \phi
        \left(
            \frac{v_t+b_t}{2}
        \right)
    \right|
    +
    \|\phi-\psi\|_\infty \\
    &\le
    \frac12
    \left|
        \langle g_t,u\rangle-v_t
    \right|
    +
    \frac{\alpha}{4}
    \le
    \frac{3\alpha}{8}
    \le
    \alpha.
\end{align*}
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
for every \(t\le n\). Hence
\begin{equation*}
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-covering-number}{\normalcolor N_\infty^{\mathrm{seq}}}}
    \left(
        \alpha,
        \Phi\circ(\tfrac12 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:affine-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal A\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]),
        \mathbf z
    \right)
    \le
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-covering-number}{\normalcolor N_\infty^{\mathrm{seq}}}}
    \left(
        \alpha/4,
         
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
],
        \mathbf g
    \right)
    |\mathcal U|.
\end{equation*}
 
 
 
 
 
 
Taking logarithms and then the supremum over $\mathbf z$ proves the claim.
<!-- end proof-10 -->

\textbf{of \cref{lem:iid-online-reduction-identity}. }
