Since the gradients are iid, the conditional
means in \eqref{eq:online_reduced_value_game} satisfy
\(m_t(u)=\langle\mu_P,u\rangle\). Consequently, the corresponding term
divided by \(T\) is
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\mathbb E\sup_{u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}
    \left\{\langle\mu_P,u\rangle-\max_{t\le T}\langle G_t,u\rangle\right\}
\circled{1}[=]
\mathbb E\sup_{u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}\inf_{\lambda\in\Delta_T}
\left\langle \sum_{t=1}^T\lambda_tG_t-\mu_P,u\right\rangle
\\
&\quad\circled{2}[=]
\mathbb E\inf_{\lambda\in\Delta_T}\sup_{u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}
\left\langle \sum_{t=1}^T\lambda_tG_t-\mu_P,u\right\rangle
\circled{3}[=]
\mathbb E\inf_{\lambda\in\Delta_T}
\left\|\sum_{t=1}^T\lambda_tG_t-\mu_P\right\|_{ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:polar-body}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[][\circ]}
\\
&= \mathbb E\operatorname{dist}_{ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:polar-body}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[][\circ]}
\bigl(\mu_P,\operatorname{conv}\{G_1,\ldots,G_T\}\bigr).
\end{aligned}
\]
Here \( 
    \IfNoValueTF{\)}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};}}{\)} } 
 renames \(u\) as \(-u\) (recall that $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$ is symmetric) and writes the minimum as an
infimum over the simplex, \( 
    \IfNoValueTF{\)}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {2};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {2};}}{\)} } 
 is von Neumann's minimax theorem, and
\( 
    \IfNoValueTF{\)}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {3};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {3};}}{\)} } 
 is the definition of the polar norm.

\textbf{of \cref{thm:iid-centered-convex-hull}. }

The lower bound follows by taking the supremum over \(\alpha>0\) in
    \cref{prop:iid-lower-bound-fat}.
For the upper bound, we may focus on zero-mean distributions. Indeed, for an arbitrary
distribution \(P\) supported on \( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\), convexity gives \(\mu_P\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\), and hence
\((G_i-\mu_P)/2\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\). The law of these centered random vectors has mean zero,
and
\[
    \operatorname{dist}_{ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:polar-body}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[][\circ]}
    \left(\mu_P,\operatorname{conv}\{G_{1:T}\}\right)
    =
    2\operatorname{dist}_{ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:polar-body}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[][\circ]}
    \left(0,\operatorname{conv}\{(G_i-\mu_P)/2:i\in[T]\}\right).
\]
Thus a uniform bound for mean-zero distributions implies the desired bound
after absorbing the factor of \(2\) into \(C\) and taking the supremum over
\(P\).

Now let \(\widetilde 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
:=\Lambda^{-1} 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\). Homogeneity gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:worst-case-convex-hull-distance}{\normalcolor \operatorname{ConvDist}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}}

It therefore suffices to prove the claim when \(\Lambda=1\). Assume this for

remains to prove the centered estimate. Using the linear class in

\[
    \Phi\circ\left(\tfrac12

and set \(\widehat P_Tf=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^T f(G_t)\). Fix

    \\

before bounding covering numbers by fat-shattering dimension, gives

Finally, \( 
    \IfNoValueTF{\)}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {4};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{

 uses \citet[Theorem~12.8]{anthony1999neural}, which
bounds fixed-design \(\ell_\infty\) covering numbers by fat-shattering
dimension up to logarithmic factors, together with
\(\eta\le\alpha+T^{-1}\) and monotonicity of the integral. We may take
\(c\le1\) and \(\gamma\ge1\). Choose \(g_0\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\) and \(u_0\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\) that
attain \( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:iid-centered-convex-hull][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]=1\). By symmetry, \(g_0\) is
\(\beta\)-shattered by \( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:iid-centered-convex-hull][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]\) for
\(0<\beta\le2\). Thus, when \(\alpha\le1/2\), the integral over
\([1/2,1]\) absorbs the term \(T^{-1}\log(e/\eta)\) from the last integral in
\( 
    \IfNoValueTF{\)}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {3};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {3};}}{\)} } 
. When \(\alpha>1/2\), that term is absorbed by \(C\alpha\).
The deterministic and entropy arguments cited above use only convexity,
symmetry, and the normalized range bound, so their proofs apply verbatim to
\( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[proof:thm:iid-centered-convex-hull][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,

]\). Taking the infimum over \(\alpha\) proves the

\(( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,

)\).

<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{prop:iid-offset}
Let \(\mathcal Z\subseteq\mathbb R^d\) be measurable, let \(P\) be a
probability distribution supported on \(\mathcal Z\) with mean \(0\), and let
\(G_1,\ldots,G_T\stackrel{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}P\). Let \(\mathcal C\) be a
nonempty pointwise-measurable class of measurable functions
\(\mathcal Z\to[0,2]\), and write
\(
    Ph:=\int h\,dP
\)
and
\(
    \widehat P_T h:= T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^T h(G_t)
\).
There are universal constants \(C,c,\gamma>0\) such that, for every
\(0<\delta\le1\),
\[
    \mathbb E\sup_{h\in\mathcal C}\bigl(Ph-2\widehat P_T h\bigr)
    \le
    C\delta+\frac{C}{T}\left[1+\int_\delta^1
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[c\alpha](\mathcal C)\log^\gamma\!\left(\frac{eT}{\alpha}\right)
    \,d\alpha\right].
\]
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
For each \(t\), draw an independent pair
\(Z_t^+,Z_t^-\stackrel{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}P\), and let
\(\varepsilon_1,\ldots,\varepsilon_T\) be independent Rademacher signs. Ghost-sample
symmetrization and a random swap within each pair give
\[
\begin{aligned}
    &\mathbb E
    \sup_{h\in\mathcal C}
    \sum_{t=1}^T\bigl(Ph-2h(G_t)\bigr)
    \le
    \mathbb E_{Z,\varepsilon}
    \sup_{h\in\mathcal C}
    \sum_{t=1}^T
    \left[
        h(Z_t^{-\varepsilon_t})
        -
        2h(Z_t^{\varepsilon_t})
    \right].
\end{aligned}
\]
Condition on the \(2T\) points \(Z_{1:T}^+,Z_{1:T}^-\). Unlike
in the adaptive case, these points do not depend on the sign history, so the
dyadic lower-approximation argument in the proof of
\cref{prop:sequential-offset} uses ordinary fixed-design \(\ell_\infty\)
covers. That argument and \cref{lem:finite-class-offset} yield
\[
\begin{aligned}
    &\mathbb E_\varepsilon
    \sup_{h\in\mathcal C}
    \frac1T\sum_{t=1}^T
    \left[
        h(Z_t^{-\varepsilon_t})
        -
        2h(Z_t^{\varepsilon_t})
    \right]
    \le
    C\delta
    +
    \frac{C}{T}
    \left[
        1
        +
        \int_\delta^1
        \log {\protect\hyperlink{def:covering-number}{\normalcolor N_\infty}}(c\alpha,\mathcal C,Z_{1:2T})
        \,d\alpha
    \right].
\end{aligned}
\]
Applying \citet[Theorem~12.8]{anthony1999neural} with sample size \(2T\) and
range bound 2 gives, we have the following bound:
\[
    \log {\protect\hyperlink{def:covering-number}{\normalcolor N_\infty}}(c\alpha,\mathcal C,2T)
    \le
    C 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[c'\alpha](\mathcal C)
    \log^\gamma\!\left(\frac{eT}{\alpha}\right).
\]
Averaging over the pairs and renaming the universal scale constant proves the
claim.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- proposition-9: body_md -->

,

[][\circ]}

[#1][#2]

<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
{
Set
\(
    m
    :=
    \min\left\{
         
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]),T
    \right\}.
\)
If \(m=0\), take \(P\) to be the point mass at \(0\). Hence, suppose
\(m\ge1\). By
\cref{fact:fat-shattering-evaluation-cubes}, there are
\(g_1,\ldots,g_m\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\) whose evaluation image contains
\((\alpha/2)B_\infty^m\). Set \(\eta:=(2T)^{-1}\), and let \(P\) assign
mass \(\eta\) to every \(g_i\) and the remaining mass to \(0\). This is a
probability distribution because \(m\le T\).

Given a sample from \(P\), let \(S\subseteq[m]\) contain the indices that
appear and set \(M:= m-|S|\). The fact that the image contains \((\alpha/2)B_\infty^m\) gives \(u_S\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\) such that \(\langle u_S,g_i\rangle=0\) for \(i\in S\) and
\(\langle u_S,g_i\rangle=\alpha/2\) otherwise. Thus every point in the
sampled convex hull has pairing zero with \(u_S\), whereas
\[
    \left\langle u_S,\mu_P\right\rangle
    =
    \frac{\alpha\eta M}{2}.
\]
Consequently, the distance from \(\mu_P\) to the sampled convex hull is at
least \(\alpha\eta M/2\). Since
\(\mathbb E M=m(1-\eta)^T\ge m/2\), taking expectations gives
\[
    \mathbb E
    \operatorname{dist}_{ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:polar-body}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[][\circ]}
    \left(\mu_P,\operatorname{conv}\{G_{1:T}\}\right)
    \ge
    \frac{\alpha m}{8T}
    =
    \frac{\alpha}{8}
    \left(
        \frac{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])}{T}
        \wedge 1
    \right).
\]
The definition of
\( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:worst-case-convex-hull-distance}{\normalcolor \operatorname{ConvDist}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\mathrm{wc}] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,T]\) now gives the final
claim.
}

<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{thm:coupon_lower_bound}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:thm:coupon_lower_bound}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        \llap{\smash{ 
        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
            \setlength\@tempdima\HyperRaiseLinkDefault
            \HyperRaiseLinkHook
        \expandafter\endgroup
        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{ 
            \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
            \hypertarget{proof:thm:coupon_lower_bound}{} 
            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
F ix \(p,q\in[1,\infty]\) and \(T\ge3\). After decreasing the universal constant, if necessary, it is
enough to consider large \(T\).  Let \(k\ge2\) be the largest integer satisfying
\(k\log k\le T\), and set \(d=T\).  Then \(k\le d\) and
\(k\asymp T/\log T\).  In the first \(k\) coordinates of \(\mathbb R^d\),
consider the centered simplex vertices
\[
    v_i := \frac12\left(e_i-\frac1k\mathbf 1\right),
\qquad i=1,\dots,k,
\]
where \(e_i\) is the \(i\)-th standard basis vector in \(\mathbb R^k\) and
\(\mathbf 1=(1,\ldots,1)\).  Let \(P\) be the uniform distribution on \(\{v_1,\ldots,v_k\}\). \(P\)
has mean \(0\), since
\(
    \frac1k\sum_{i=1}^k v_i
    =
    \frac12\left(\frac1k\mathbf 1-\frac1k\mathbf 1\right)
    =
    0.
\)
Moreover, the distribution \(P\) is supported on
\(B_{q^\ast}^d\), because
\(
    \|v_i\|_{q^\ast}
    \le
    \|v_i\|_1
    =
    1-\frac1k
    <1.
\)

Draw \(G_1,\ldots,G_T\stackrel{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}P\), and write
\[
    S:= \{i\in[k]: v_i \text{ appears among } G_1,\ldots,G_T\}.
\]
If some index \(j\) is missing, then every
\(y\in\operatorname{conv}\{v_i:i\in S\}\) has \(j\)-th coordinate
\(
    y_j=-\frac1{2k},
\)
since \((v_i)_j=-1/(2k)\) for all \(i\neq j\). Consequently, for every
\(p\in[1,\infty]\),
\[
    \operatorname{dist}_{p^\ast}\bigl(0,\operatorname{conv}\{G_1,\ldots,G_T\}\bigr)
    =
    \inf_{y\in\operatorname{conv}\{G_1,\ldots,G_T\}}\|y\|_{p^\ast}
    \ge
    \frac1{2k}
    \qquad\text{on the event } \{S\neq[k]\}.
\]

It remains to show that this event has probability bounded below by a universal
constant.  Let \(M:= k-|S|\) be the number of missing support points.  If
\(X_i\) is the indicator that \(v_i\) is not sampled, then \(M=\sum_i X_i\),
and the standard second-moment computation gives
\[
    \mathbb E M
    =
    k\Big(1-\frac1k\Big)^T,
    \qquad
    \mathbb E M^2
    =
    \mathbb E M
    +
    k(k-1)\Big(1-\frac2k\Big)^T .
\]
The maximality of \(k\) gives \(k\log k\le T<(k+1)\log(k+1)\).  Hence
\((1-\frac1k)^T\asymp k^{-1}\) and
\((1-\frac2k)^T\lesssim k^{-2}\), with universal constants.  Therefore
there are universal constants \(0<c_1<c_2<\infty\) such that
\(
    c_1\le \mathbb E M\le c_2,
    \mathbb E M^2\le c_2,
\)
Combining the geometric observation above with the Paley-Zygmund inequality,
and using \(k\asymp T/\log T\), yields
\[
    \mathbb E\operatorname{dist}_{p^\ast}
    \bigl(0,\operatorname{conv}\{G_1,\ldots,G_T\}\bigr)
    \circled{1}[\geq]
    \frac{1}{2k}\mathbb P(M>0)
    \circled{2}[\ge]
    \frac{(\mathbb E M)^2}{2k\mathbb E M^2}
    \ge
    \frac{c_0}{k} \geq \frac{c\log T}{T}.
\]
Here \( 
    \IfNoValueTF{\)}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};}}{\)} } 
 is the geometric observation above and \( 
    \IfNoValueTF{\)}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {2};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {2};}}{\)} } 

is the Paley-Zygmund inequality. The constants \(c_0,c>0\) are universal.
<!-- end proof-15 -->

S et
\[
    a:=\left(\frac1q-\frac1p\right)_+,
    \qquad
    s:=
    a+\frac1p-\left(\frac1q-\frac12\right)_+.
\]
Then \(

[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]=d^a\).
If \(p=q=\infty\), the endpoint ordinary fat-shattering profile,
\cref{thm:iid-centered-convex-hull,prop:iid-lower-bound-fat}, and the trivial
diameter bound give
\[

[#1][\mathrm{wc}] 
[\infty,1,T]
    =

\]
which proves both claims at this endpoint. Hence, assume
\((p,q)\ne(\infty,\infty)\), so \(s>0\).

First suppose \(d\ge T\). The ordinary fat-shattering bounds

\]
For the upper bound, choose \(\alpha=

[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]T^{-s}\) in

on this interval and the extra logarithm when \(s=1\))
\[
    \int_\alpha^{

[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]}

[p,q^\ast][d])
    \log^\gamma\!\left(\frac{eT

[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]}{\beta}\right)
    \,d\beta

[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]T^{1-s}\log^{\gamma+2}(eTd),
\]
and hence
\(

[#1][\mathrm{wc}] 
[p,q^\ast,T]
    =
    \widetilde O_{p,q}( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]T^{-s})
\).
For the lower bound, choose
\(\alpha\asymp_{p,q} 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]T^{-s}\) sufficiently small
that \cref{eq:fat_shattering_values,eq:fat-shattering-11} give

[#1][#2]

We only need a constant fraction of \(T\), including when \(d=T\).
Applying \cref{prop:iid-lower-bound-fat} therefore yields

[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])}{T}\wedge1\right)

[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]T^{-s}.

Finally,
\[

[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]T^{-s}
    =
    \left(\frac dT\right)^a
    \frac{1}{T^{\frac1p-(\frac1q-\frac12)_+}},
\]
which proves the first claim.

Suppose now that \(d\le T\).
Write \(\rho:= 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:rho-profile}{\normalcolor \rho_{p,q,d}\IfBlankF{def:rho-profile}{(def:rho-profile)}}} 
[d]\), with \( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:rho-profile}{\normalcolor \rho_{p,q,d}\IfBlankF{def:rho-profile}{(def:rho-profile)}}} 
\) as in
\cref{eq:rho-pqd}. We first prove
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:worst-case-convex-hull-distance}{\normalcolor \operatorname{ConvDist}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\mathrm{wc}] 
[p,q^\ast,T]
    =
    \widetilde{\Theta}_{p,q}\!\left(\frac{d\rho}{T}\right).
\]

For the upper bound, the ordinary fat-shattering estimates of
\citet{mendelson2004shattering} and
\citet{guzman2015information}, summarized in the related-work discussion
and supplemented by \eqref{eq:fat-shattering-11} at \((1,1)\),
give
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\beta]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \lesssim_{p,q}
    \min\left\{
        d,
        L_\beta\left(\frac{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]}{\beta}\right)^{1/s}
    \right\},
\]
and \(\rho= 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]d^{-s}\). Apply
\cref{thm:iid-centered-convex-hull} with \(\alpha=\rho/T\). Splitting its
integral at \(\rho\) gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
    \int_\alpha^{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]}
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[c\beta]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \log^\gamma\!\left(\frac{eT 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]}{\beta}\right)
    \,d\beta
    &\lesssim_{p,q}
    d\rho\,\log^{\gamma+2}(eTd).
\end{aligned}
\]
Indeed, on \([\alpha,\rho]\) use \( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[c\beta]\le d\), which contributes at
most \(d\rho\) times the logarithmic factor. On
\([\rho, 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]]\), use
\(L_{c\beta}\lesssim_{p,q}\log(eTd)\). Integrating
\(( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]/\beta)^{1/s}\) gives \(O_{p,q}( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][1/s]\rho^{1-1/s}) =  O_{p,q}(d\rho)\) when \(s<1\)
and one additional logarithm when \(s=1\). Since \(0<s\le1\) and
\(\rho= 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]d^{-s}\), we also have
\( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]\le d\rho\), so the theorem yields the claimed upper
bound.

For the lower bound, for all sufficiently large \(d\), choose an
integer \(m\asymp_{p,q}d\) with \(m\le c_{p,q}d\) in
\cref{lem:evaluation_cubes}. Since \( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:rho-profile}{\normalcolor \rho_{p,q,d}\IfBlankF{def:rho-profile}{(def:rho-profile)}}} 
[m]\) is nonincreasing in
\(m\), cf. \cref{eq:rho-pqd}, the resulting cube has radius
\(R\gtrsim_{p,q} 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:rho-profile}{\normalcolor \rho_{p,q,d}\IfBlankF{def:rho-profile}{(def:rho-profile)}}} 
[d]=\rho\). For the finitely many remaining
dimensions, the one-coordinate cube gives the same conclusion after
decreasing the \((p,q)\)-dependent constant. By
\cref{fact:fat-shattering-evaluation-cubes}, this gives
\( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[2R]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])\ge m\). Since \(m\le d\le T\),
\cref{prop:iid-lower-bound-fat} with \(\alpha=2R\) gives
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:worst-case-convex-hull-distance}{\normalcolor \operatorname{ConvDist}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][\mathrm{wc}] 
[p,q^\ast,T]
    \ge
    \frac{\alpha}{8}
    \left(
        \frac{ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])}{T}
        \wedge 1
    \right)
    \ge
    \frac{Rm}{4T}
    \gtrsim_{p,q}
    \frac{d\rho}{T}.
\]
Finally, substituting \(m\asymp_{p,q}d\) into \cref{eq:rho-pqd} and using
\[
    \frac1q-\frac1{\max\{q,2\}}
    =
    \left(\frac1q-\frac12\right)_+
\]
gives the expression in the statement in both regimes.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{cor:explicit_regimes}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:cor:explicit_regimes}{}

T he \(p<q\) and \(p\ge q\) cases follow by combining the corresponding upper
bound in \cref{thm:norm-specific-upper-bound} and lower bound in
\cref{thm:norm-specific-lower-bound}, both proved below.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
\label{def:uniform-convexity-and-range}
Let $(E,\|\cdot\|)$ be a normed space, and  \(K\subseteq E\) be convex.  
For \(c>0\) and \(\kappa\ge2\), a function
\(\Phi:K\to\mathbb R\) is \emph{\((c,\kappa)\)-uniformly convex on \(K\)}
with respect to \(\|\cdot\|\) if
\[
    \frac{\Phi(u)+\Phi(v)}2
    \ge
    \Phi\!\left(\frac{u+v}{2}\right)
    +c\|u-v\|^\kappa,
    \qquad u,v\in K.
\]
Its range on \(K\) is
\(
    \operatorname{range}_K(\Phi)
    :=
    \sup_{u\in K}\Phi(u)-\inf_{u\in K}\Phi(u).
\)
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:uniformly-convex-localization}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:lem:uniformly-convex-localization}{$\downarrow$}}]}
Let \(\|\cdot\|\) and suppose \(\|g\|_*\le L\) for every
\(g\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\).
Let \(K\subseteq\mathbb R^d\) be convex, and let \(\Phi:K\to\mathbb R\) be
\((c,\kappa)\)-uniformly convex on \(K\) with respect to \(\|\cdot\|\) and range \(D\). Then
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[K, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])
    \le
    \frac{D}{c}\left(\frac{L}{\alpha}\right)^\kappa .
\]
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:seq_union_bound}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:lem:seq_union_bound}{$\downarrow$}}]}
Let \(\mathcal G_1,\ldots,\mathcal G_N\) be real-valued function classes on
the same domain, and set
\(
\mathcal G:= \bigcup_{i=1}^N \mathcal G_i .
\)
If
\(
 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha](\mathcal G_i)\le m
\text{ for every }i\in[N],
\)
then
\(
 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha](\mathcal G)
\le
C\bigl(m+\log N\bigr),
\)
for a universal constant \(C\).
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{lem:seq_union_bound}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:lem:seq_union_bound}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        \llap{\smash{ 
        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
            \setlength\@tempdima\HyperRaiseLinkDefault
            \HyperRaiseLinkHook
        \expandafter\endgroup
        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{ 
            \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
            \hypertarget{proof:lem:seq_union_bound}{} 
            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
F or a subset of functions \(\mathcal V\subseteq\mathcal G_i\), write
\(\mathrm{rk}(\mathcal V):= 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha](\mathcal V)\), with
\(\mathrm{rk}(\varnothing)=-1\). We will have an expert predicting on each subset, and once the subset is empty the corresponding expert
predicts arbitrarily. At a node with instance-threshold pair \((x,s)\), define
\[
    \mathcal V_+
    :=
    \{f\in\mathcal V:f(x)\ge s+\alpha/2\},
    \qquad
    \mathcal V_-
    :=
    \{f\in\mathcal V:f(x)\le s-\alpha/2\}.
\]
If \(\mathrm{rk}(\mathcal V)=R\), the two children cannot both have rank at
least \(R\), since otherwise attaching their depth-\(R\) shattered trees below \((x,s)\)
would give a depth-\((R+1)\) tree shattered by \(\mathcal V\). Hence
\[
    \min\{\mathrm{rk}(\mathcal V_+),\mathrm{rk}(\mathcal V_-)\}
    \le R-1.
\]
The rank algorithm predicts the sign of the child with larger rank, breaking
ties arbitrarily, and updates to the child selected by the observed sign. On
an \(\alpha\)-realizable path, every mistaken prediction therefore decreases
the rank by at least one, and thus the rank algorithm for \(\mathcal G_i\) makes at most
\( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha](\mathcal G_i)\le m\) mistakes.

Now run the \(N\) rank algorithms as experts. Give every expert initial
weight \(1\), predict by weighted majority, and after each round multiply the
weight of each mistaken expert by \(1/2\). If the master makes a mistake, then
at least half of the current total weight is on mistaken experts, and hence
the total weight $Z_t$ is multiplied by at most \(3/4\). After \(M\) master
    mistakes, we have $ 
    \IfNoValueTF{$}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {2};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {2};}}{$} } 
 below, where $ 
    \IfNoValueTF{$}{ \tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};} }{ \stackrel{\tikz[baseline=(char.base)]{
    \node[shape=circle,draw,inner sep=0.8pt] (char) {1};}}{$} } 
 holds if the sequence is \(\alpha\)-realizable by \(\mathcal G_i\), since expert \(i\)
makes at most \(m\) mistakes and thus its final weight is at least \(2^{-m}\): 
\[
2^{-m}
\circled{1}[\le]
Z_T
\circled{2}[\le]
    N\left(\frac34\right)^M, \qquad \text{ and hence} \qquad
M
\le
\frac{m+\log_2 N}{-\log_2(3/4)}
\le
4(m+\log_2 N).
\]

Finally, let \(n\) be the depth of an arbitrary tree shattered by
\(\mathcal G\), and run the \(N\) rank algorithms and their weighted-majority
master at its nodes.
The two edges below each node represent the two possible signs of the margin
constraint at that node. After the master predicts one sign, the adversary selects the edge
with the other sign. Repeating this rule constructs a root-to-leaf path on
which the master makes exactly \(n\) mistakes. Since the tree is shattered,
    this complete path is witnessed by some function \(f\in\mathcal G\), and
the union representation \(\mathcal G=\bigcup_{i=1}^N\mathcal G_i\) places
that function in some \(\mathcal G_i\). The mistake bound above therefore
applies to the constructed path and yields
\(n\le4(m+\log_2N)\), proving the lemma.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:barycentric_covering_bound}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:cor:barycentric_covering_bound}{$\downarrow$}}]}
Let \(1<r<\infty\) and suppose
\(B_{q^{\ast}}^d\subseteq L B_{r^{\ast}}^d\). Then
\[
 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
\lesssim_r
\inf_{R>0}
\left[
\left(\frac{LR}{\alpha}\right)^{\max\{2,r\}}
+
\log N(B_p^d,RB_r^d)
\right].
\]
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{cor:barycentric_covering_bound}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:cor:barycentric_covering_bound}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        \llap{\smash{ 
        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
            \setlength\@tempdima\HyperRaiseLinkDefault
            \HyperRaiseLinkHook
        \expandafter\endgroup
        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{ 
            \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
            \hypertarget{proof:cor:barycentric_covering_bound}{} 
            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
F ix \(R>0\), and cover \(B_p^d\) by
\[
B_p^d\subseteq \bigcup_{i=1}^N(a_i+RB_r^d),
\qquad
N=N(B_p^d,RB_r^d).
\]
Set
\[
C_i:= B_p^d\cap(a_i+RB_r^d),
\qquad
\mathcal F_i:=
\{B_{q^\ast}^d\ni g\mapsto \langle w,g\rangle:w\in C_i\}.
\]
Since \(C_i\subseteq a_i+RB_r^d\), the class \(\mathcal F_i\) is contained in
the corresponding local ball class. Moreover, shifting the ball by $a_i$ does not change $ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
$. 
Thus, using
\(
 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d]
\subseteq
\bigcup_{i=1}^N \mathcal F_i,
\)
\cref{lem:uniformly-convex-localization} and \cref{lem:seq_union_bound}, we have
\[
 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
\lesssim_r
\left(\frac{LR}{\alpha}\right)^{\max\{2,r\}}
    +\log N(B_p^d,RB_r^d) \quad \text{ for all } R > 0.
\]
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- fact-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:entropy-inputs}
For \(1\le p,s\le\infty\) and \(R>0\),
\begin{align}
    \log N(B_p^d,RB_s^d)
    &\lesssim_{p,s}
    d\left(1+\log_+\frac{d^{1/s-1/p}}R\right),
    \label{eq:volumetric-entropy-input}
    \\
    \log N(B_p^d,RB_s^d)
    &\lesssim_{p,s}
    R^{-1/(1/p-1/s)}\log\frac{Ced}{R},
    \qquad 1\le p<s\le2,\quad 0<R<1.
    \label{eq:schutt-entropy-input}
\end{align}
<!-- end fact-1 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:norm-specific-upper-bound}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:thm:norm-specific-upper-bound}{$\downarrow$}}]}
For every \(1\le p,q\le\infty\), \((p,q)\ne(\infty,\infty)\), and
\(\alpha>0\),
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \lesssim_{p,q}
    L_\alpha
    \begin{cases}
    \displaystyle
    \min\left\{
        \alpha^{-\left(\frac{1}{p}-(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2})_+\right)^{-1}},
        \ d\left(
            1+\log_+
            \frac{1}{\alpha d^{\frac{1}{p}-(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2})_+}}
        \right)
    \right\},
    & p < q,\\[4mm]
    \displaystyle
    \min\left\{
        \left(\frac{d^{1/q-1/p}}{\alpha}\right)^{\max\{q,2\}},
        \ d\left(
            1+\log_+
            \frac{d^{1/q-1/p}}{\alpha d^{1/\max\{q,2\}}}
        \right)
    \right\},
    & p \geq q.
    \end{cases}
\]
At \(p=q=\infty\),
\(
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\infty,1][d])
    \lesssim
    d\left(1+\log_+\frac1\alpha\right).
\)
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{thm:norm-specific-upper-bound}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:thm:norm-specific-upper-bound}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        \llap{\smash{ 
        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
            \setlength\@tempdima\HyperRaiseLinkDefault
            \HyperRaiseLinkHook
        \expandafter\endgroup
        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{ 
            \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
            \hypertarget{proof:thm:norm-specific-upper-bound}{} 
            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
W hen \(p<q\), every function in the class takes values in \([-1,1]\). We can restrict to \(0<\alpha\le 1\). Indeed, since \( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]\) is nonincreasing in \(\alpha\), we can map the case \(1<\alpha\le2\) to the case $\alpha =1$, which just changes the constant.
If \(\alpha>2\), even a depth-one shattered
tree would require two function values separated by at least \(\alpha\),
which is impossible for a class taking values in \([-1,1]\), so $ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]$ would be $0$.

\@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
   {1.5ex plus 0.5ex minus .2ex} 
   {-1em} 
   {\normalfont\normalsize\rmfamily\bfseries} 
{The case \(p < q\) and \(p\ge2\).}
Apply
\cref{cor:barycentric_covering_bound} with \(r=p\) and \(L=1\).
Taking \(R=1\), for which \(N(B_p^d,B_p^d)=1\), gives \( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])\lesssim_p\alpha^{-p}\).
Using \eqref{eq:volumetric-entropy-input} instead and taking
\(R=\alpha d^{1/p}\) gives
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \lesssim_p
    d\left(1+\log_+\frac1{\alpha d^{1/p}}\right).
\]
\@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
   {1.5ex plus 0.5ex minus .2ex} 
   {-1em} 
   {\normalfont\normalsize\rmfamily\bfseries} 
{The case \(p < q\) and \(p<2\).}
Set
\[
    s:=\min\{2,q\},
    \qquad
    b:=\frac1p-\frac1s.
\]
Then \(p<s\le2\), \(B_{q^{\ast}}^d\subseteq B_{s^{\ast}}^d\), and
\(\theta=b+1/2\). Applying \cref{cor:barycentric_covering_bound} with
\(r=s\) and \(L=1\), followed by \eqref{eq:schutt-entropy-input}, gives for
every \(0<R<1\)
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \lesssim_{p,q}
    \frac{R^2}{\alpha^2}
    +R^{-1/b}\log\frac{Ced}{R}.
\]
For \(0<\alpha<1\), take \(R=\alpha^{2b/(2b+1)}\). Since
\(\log(Ced/R)\lesssim_{p,q}L_\alpha\), we obtain
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \lesssim_{p,q}
    L_\alpha\alpha^{-1/\theta}.
\]
The case \(\alpha=1\) follows by monotonicity.
For the $d$-dependent term, use \eqref{eq:volumetric-entropy-input} in
\cref{cor:barycentric_covering_bound} with \(r=s\), \(L=1\), and
\(R=\alpha\sqrt d\). Since \(\theta=1/p-1/s+1/2\), this gives
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \lesssim_{p,q}
    d\left(1+\log_+\frac1{\alpha d^\theta}\right).
\]
Since
\(
\theta= b + 1/2 = 1/p-(1/q-1/2)_+,
\)
we have the result.

\@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
   {1.5ex plus 0.5ex minus .2ex} 
   {-1em} 
   {\normalfont\normalsize\rmfamily\bfseries} 
{The case \(p \ge q\) and \(q<\infty\).}
Set
\(
    r:=\max\{q,2\}
\)
 and 
\(
    A:= d^{1/q-1/p}.
\)

If \(q\ge2\), use \(r=q\), \(L=1\),
and \(R=A\), the inclusion \(B_p^d\subseteq A B_q^d\) makes the covering
number equal to one and gives
\(
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \lesssim_q
    \left(\frac A\alpha\right)^q.
\)

If \(q<2\) and \(p>1\), set \(u=\min\{p,2\}\). The inclusions
\[
    B_p^d\subseteq d^{1/u-1/p}B_u^d,
    \qquad
    B_{q^\ast}^d\subseteq d^{1/q-1/u}B_{u^\ast}^d
\]
have product distortion \(A\). Applying the same covering bound with
\(r=u\) and a one-element cover therefore gives
\(
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \lesssim_{p,q}
    \left(\frac A\alpha\right)^2.
\)

For \(p=q=1\), it suffices to consider the nontrivial range
\(\alpha\le2\). Take \(u=1+1/L_\alpha\). Then
\(B_1^d\subseteq B_u^d\), \(B_\infty^d\subseteq eB_{u^\ast}^d\), and the
quadratic modulus constant from \citet{ball1994sharp} is of order \(u-1\).
Thus the same argument gives
\(
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[1,\infty][d])
    \lesssim
    L_\alpha\alpha^{-2}.
\)

For the dimension dependent term, set
\(
    \lambda:= d^{1/r^\ast-1/q^\ast}=d^{1/q-1/r}.
\)
Then \(B_{q^\ast}^d\subseteq\lambda B_{r^\ast}^d\). Hence
\cref{cor:barycentric_covering_bound} and
\eqref{eq:volumetric-entropy-input} give
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]
    \lesssim_{p,q}
    \inf_{R>0}
    \left[
        \left(\frac{\lambda R}{\alpha}\right)^r
        +d\left(1+\log_+\frac{d^{1/r-1/p}}R\right)
    \right].
\]
Taking \(R=\alpha\lambda^{-1}d^{1/r}\) makes the first term equal to \(d\),
and
\(
    \lambda d^{-1/p}=A d^{-1/r}.
\)
Thus
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]
    \lesssim_{p,q}
    d\left(1+\log_+\frac{A}{\alpha d^{1/r}}\right).
\]

\@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
   {1.5ex plus 0.5ex minus .2ex} 
   {-1em} 
   {\normalfont\normalsize\rmfamily\bfseries} 
{The case \(p=q=\infty\).}
Apply \eqref{eq:volumetric-entropy-input} to an
\(\alpha/4\)-net of \(B_\infty^d\) in \(\ell_\infty\). 
Let \(\mathcal V\) be such a net. For every \(w\in B_\infty^d\), choose
\(v\in\mathcal V\) with \(\lVert w-v\rVert_\infty\le\alpha/4\). Uniformly over
\(g\in B_1^d\), the corresponding linear functions then differ by at most
\(\alpha/4\). Replacing the witnesses of an \(\alpha\)-shattered tree by
their nearest net points therefore gives an \((\alpha/2)\)-shattered tree for
the finite class indexed by \(\mathcal V\). A depth-\(n\) shattered tree
requires a distinct function for each of its \(2^n\) root-to-leaf paths, so
\(n\le\log_2|\mathcal V|\). Finally,
\eqref{eq:volumetric-entropy-input} bounds this logarithm by
\(O\bigl(d(1+\log_+(1/\alpha))\bigr)\). Thus
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\infty,1][d])
    \lesssim
    d\left(1+\log_+\frac1\alpha\right).
\]
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- fact-2: body_md -->
\label{fact:fat-shattering-evaluation-cubes}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:fact:fat-shattering-evaluation-cubes}{$\downarrow$}}]}
Let \( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\subseteq\mathbb R^d\) be centrally symmetric convex bodies, let
\(m\in\mathbb N\), and let \(g_1,\ldots,g_m\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\). For every \(\alpha>0\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:equivalence_of_fat_shattering_to_cubes}
    \{g_1,\ldots,g_m\}\text{ is \(\alpha\)-shattered by }
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]
    \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
    \frac{\alpha}{2}B_\infty^m
    \subseteq
    \EvalCube[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]{g_{1:m}}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end fact-2 -->
<!-- fact-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:evaluation_cubes}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:lem:evaluation_cubes}{$\downarrow$}}]}
There is a constant \(c_{p,q}>0\) such that, for every
\(1\le m\le c_{p,q}d\), there exist
\(g_1,\dots,g_m\in B_{q^{\ast}}^d\) satisfying
\[
\left\{
(\langle w,g_1\rangle,\dots,\langle w,g_m\rangle):
w\in B_p^d
\right\}
\supseteq
c_{p,q}{ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:rho-profile}{\normalcolor \rho_{p,q,d}\IfBlankF{def:rho-profile}{(def:rho-profile)}}} 
[m]}[-1,1]^m .
\]
<!-- end fact-3 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:cube_to_tree}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:lem:cube_to_tree}{$\downarrow$}}]}
Let \( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\subseteq\mathbb R^d\) be centrally symmetric convex bodies and let \(g_1,\ldots,g_m\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\). We have
\[
    R B_\infty^m\subseteq \EvalCube[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]{g_{1:m}} \qquad \implies \qquad
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])
    \ge
    m\left\lfloor\log_2\left(1+\frac{2R}{\alpha}\right)\right\rfloor.
\]
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{lem:cube_to_tree}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:lem:cube_to_tree}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        \llap{\smash{ 
        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
            \setlength\@tempdima\HyperRaiseLinkDefault
            \HyperRaiseLinkHook
        \expandafter\endgroup
        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{ 
            \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
            \hypertarget{proof:lem:cube_to_tree}{} 
            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
A single interval \([-R,R]\) supports the usual binary-search tree. For a
current interval \([a,b]\), use the midpoint \(s=(a+b)/2\) as threshold. The
minus child retains \([a,s-\alpha/2]\), and the plus child retains
\([s+\alpha/2,b]\). After \(t\) levels, every surviving interval has length
\(
    2^{-t}(2R+\alpha)-\alpha.
\)
It is therefore nonempty through
\(
    h:=
    \left\lfloor\log_2\left(1+\frac{2R}{\alpha}\right)\right\rfloor
\)
levels. Every point in a terminal interval satisfies the margin inequalities
along the corresponding branch.

Concatenate this construction in \(m\) blocks. In block \(j\), label every
node by \(g_j\) and use the binary-search thresholds for coordinate \(j\).
A branch of depth \(mh\) determines terminal intervals
\(I_1,\dots,I_m\subseteq[-R,R]\). Choose
\(
    z_j\in I_j, j\in[m].
\)
By the cube containment, for a \(u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\), we have
\[
    \langle u,g_j\rangle=z_j,
    \qquad j\in[m].
\]
This witness satisfies every margin constraint on the branch, so the
concatenated tree is \(\alpha\)-shattered to depth \(mh\).
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:norm-specific-lower-bound}{\normalfont[{\hyperlink{proof:thm:norm-specific-lower-bound}{$\downarrow$}}]}
Let \(1\le p,q\le\infty\), \((p,q)\ne(\infty,\infty)\), and assume
\(0<\alpha\le2 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]\). Then
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \gtrsim_{p,q}
    \begin{cases}
    \displaystyle
    \min\left\{
        \alpha^{-\left(\frac{1}{p}-(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2})_+\right)^{-1}},
        \ d\left(
            1+\log_+
            \frac{1}{\alpha d^{\frac{1}{p}-(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2})_+}}
        \right)
    \right\},
    & p < q,\\[4mm]
    \displaystyle
    \min\left\{
        \left(\frac{d^{1/q-1/p}}{\alpha}\right)^{\max\{q,2\}},
        \ d\left(
            1+\log_+
            \frac{d^{1/q-1/p}}{\alpha d^{1/\max\{q,2\}}}
        \right)
    \right\},
    & p \geq q.
    \end{cases}
\]
For \(p=q=\infty\) and \(0<\alpha\le2\),
\(
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\infty,1][d])
    \gtrsim
    d\left(1+\log_+\frac1\alpha\right).
\)
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{thm:norm-specific-lower-bound}. }

        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
F irst assume $0<\alpha\le c_{p,q} 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]$ for a sufficiently small constant $0<c_{p,q}\le1$.
Fix \(1\le m\le c_{p,q}d\). By
\cref{lem:evaluation_cubes}, there are \(g_1,\ldots,g_m\in B_{q^\ast}^d\)
whose evaluation image contains a cube of radius
\(c_{p,q} 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:rho-profile}{\normalcolor \rho_{p,q,d}\IfBlankF{def:rho-profile}{(def:rho-profile)}}} 
[m]\). Applying \cref{lem:cube_to_tree} with
\(( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
)=(B_p^d,B_{q^\ast}^d)\), and then taking the supremum over such
\(m\), gives the profile bound below with the supremum restricted to
\(m\le c_{p,q}d\). For \(m>c_{p,q}d\), choose
\(m'\le c_{p,q}d\) with \(m'\asymp_{p,q}m\). Since
\( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:rho-profile}{\normalcolor \rho_{p,q,d}\IfBlankF{def:rho-profile}{(def:rho-profile)}}} 
\) is nonincreasing, the profile term at \(m'\) is at least a
constant multiple of the term at \(m\). The finitely many dimensions for
which such an \(m'\) does not exist are absorbed into the constants.
Consequently,
\[
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast][d])
    \gtrsim_{p,q}
    \sup_{1\le m\le d}m
    \left\lfloor
        \log_2\left(
            1+\frac{c_{p,q} 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:rho-profile}{\normalcolor \rho_{p,q,d}\IfBlankF{def:rho-profile}{(def:rho-profile)}}} 
[m]}{\alpha}
        \right)
    \right\rfloor.
\]

We evaluate this profile directly. Fix \(K>0\), \(A>0\), and
\(0<\beta\le1\), and write \(x=A/\alpha\). On the nontrivial range
\(0<\alpha\lesssim_K A\), choosing \(m\) comparable to
\(\min\{x^{1/\beta},d\}\) gives
\[
    \sup_{1\le m\le d}
    m\left\lfloor
        \log_2\left(1+Kxm^{-\beta}\right)
    \right\rfloor
    \gtrsim_{\beta,K}
    \min\left\{
        x^{1/\beta},
        \ d\left(1+\log_+\frac{x}{d^\beta}\right)
    \right\}.
\]
Indeed, if \(x<d^\beta\), choose \(m\asymp_{\beta,K}x^{1/\beta}\), so the
logarithm is bounded below by a positive constant. If \(x\ge d^\beta\),
choose \(m\) to be a sufficiently small fixed multiple of \(d\), depending
on \(\beta,K\). This yields the second term. Adjusting the comparison
constants absorbs the floor.
 
First consider \(p=q=\infty\). Then
\(\rho_{\infty,\infty,d}(m)=1\), and taking \(m=d\) gives
\(d(1+\log_+(1/\alpha))\) up to universal constants.
Otherwise, apply the estimate with \(K=c_{p,q}\) to
the two cases in
\eqref{eq:rho-pqd}. We obtain the result with:
\[
    (A,\beta)
    =
    \left(
        1,
        \frac1p-\left(\frac1q-\frac12\right)_+
    \right)
\ \text{ for } \ p<q, \ \text{ and } \
    (A,\beta)
    =
    \left(
        d^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{p}},
        \frac1{\max\{q,2\}}
    \right)
\ \text{ for } \ p \geq q.
\]
For $c_{p,q} 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]<\alpha\le2 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]$ and
$(p,q)\ne(\infty,\infty)$, the claimed lower bound is $O_{p,q}(1)$,
whereas $ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]\ge1$: choose $g\in B_{q^\ast}^d$ and $u\in B_p^d$
attaining $\langle u,g\rangle= 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:cross-duality-constant}{\normalcolor \Lambda\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[p,q^\ast]$, and use $\pm u$ with threshold zero.
At $p=q=\infty$, coordinate evaluations of $B_\infty^d$ give
$ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]\ge d$ for every $0<\alpha\le2$, which also covers the remaining scales.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{lem:uniformly-convex-localization}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:lem:uniformly-convex-localization}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        \llap{\smash{ 
        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
            \setlength\@tempdima\HyperRaiseLinkDefault
            \HyperRaiseLinkHook
        \expandafter\endgroup
        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{ 
            \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
            \hypertarget{proof:lem:uniformly-convex-localization}{} 
            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
S uppose the class \(\alpha\)-shatters a depth-\(n\) tree, and choose a
witness \(w_\varepsilon\in K\) for every leaf \(\varepsilon\). For a node
\(u\), let \(L(u)\) be the leaves below it and set
\[
    m_u:=\frac1{|L(u)|}\sum_{\varepsilon\in L(u)}w_\varepsilon.
\]
Convexity of \(K\) gives \(m_u\in K\). If \(g_u\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\) and \(s_u\)
are the instance and threshold at an internal node, averaging the shattering
inequalities over its two child subtrees gives
\[
    \langle m_{u+},g_u\rangle\ge s_u+\frac\alpha2,
    \qquad
    \langle m_{u-},g_u\rangle\le s_u-\frac\alpha2.
\]
Consequently,
\[
    \|m_{u+}-m_{u-}\|
    \ge
    \frac{\langle m_{u+}-m_{u-},g_u\rangle}{\|g_u\|_*}
    \ge
    \frac\alpha L.
\]

For each level \(t\), define
\[
    \overline\Phi_t:= 2^{-t}\sum_{|u|=t}\Phi(m_u).
\]
Since \(m_u=(m_{u+}+m_{u-})/2\), uniform convexity yields
\[
    \overline\Phi_{t+1}
    \ge
    \overline\Phi_t+c\left(\frac\alpha L\right)^\kappa.
\]
Thus \(nc(\alpha/L)^\kappa\le
\overline\Phi_n-\overline\Phi_0\le D\), which proves the lemma.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{fact:fat-shattering-evaluation-cubes}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:fact:fat-shattering-evaluation-cubes}{} 
    \else

            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
F irst suppose that
\(\frac{\alpha}{2}B_\infty^m\subseteq \EvalCube[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]{g_{1:m}}\). For every
\(\varepsilon\in\{\pm1\}^m\), choose \(u_\varepsilon\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
\) whose evaluation
vector is \((\alpha/2)\varepsilon\). The corresponding functions shatter
\(g_1,\ldots,g_m\) with all thresholds equal to zero.

Conversely, suppose that \(g_1,\ldots,g_m\) are \(\alpha\)-shattered with
threshold vector \(s\in\mathbb R^m\), and write
\(\mathcal C=\EvalCube[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]{g_{1:m}}\). Fix \(a\in\mathbb R^m\), and choose the two
shattering functions corresponding to the sign patterns of \(a\) and \(-a\),
with arbitrary signs in the zero coordinates. Their evaluation vectors
\(y^+,y^-\in \mathcal C\) satisfy
\[
    \langle a,y^+\rangle
    \ge
    \langle a,s\rangle+\frac{\alpha}{2}\|a\|_1,
    \qquad
    \langle-a,y^-\rangle
    \ge
    \langle-a,s\rangle+\frac{\alpha}{2}\|a\|_1.
\]
Since \(\mathcal C\) is centrally symmetric, its support function satisfies
\[
    h_{\mathcal C}(a)=h_{\mathcal C}(-a)
    \ge
    \frac{\alpha}{2}\|a\|_1+|\langle a,s\rangle|
    \ge
    \frac{\alpha}{2}\|a\|_1.
\]
The rightmost expression is the support function of
\((\alpha/2)B_\infty^m\). Both sets are closed and convex, so support-function
duality gives
\((\alpha/2)B_\infty^m\subseteq \mathcal C\). This cube spans \(\mathbb R^m\), so the
evaluation map \(u\mapsto(\langle u,g_i\rangle)_{i=1}^m\) has rank \(m\),
which also proves that the \(g_i\) are linearly independent.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{lem:evaluation_cubes}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:lem:evaluation_cubes}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        \llap{\smash{ 
        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
            \setlength\@tempdima\HyperRaiseLinkDefault
            \HyperRaiseLinkHook
        \expandafter\endgroup
        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{ 
            \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
            \hypertarget{proof:lem:evaluation_cubes}{} 
            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
G iven \(g_1,\dots,g_m\), define \(T:\mathbb R^m\to\mathbb R^d\) by
\(Ta=\sum_{i=1}^ma_ig_i\), and let \(\mathcal C\) be the evaluation image in the
statement. Its support function is
\[
    \sup_{y\in \mathcal C}\langle a,y\rangle
    =
    \sup_{w\in B_p^d}\langle w,Ta\rangle
    =
    \|Ta\|_{p^\ast}.
\]
Thus it suffices in every case to prove
\(\|Ta\|_{p^\ast}\gtrsim_{p,q} 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:rho-profile}{\normalcolor \rho_{p,q,d}\IfBlankF{def:rho-profile}{(def:rho-profile)}}} 
[m]\|a\|_1\).

\@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
   {1.5ex plus 0.5ex minus .2ex} 
   {-1em} 
   {\normalfont\normalsize\rmfamily\bfseries} 
{The range \(p\le q\).}
If \(q\ge2\), take \(g_i=e_i\). Then
\[
    \|Ta\|_{p^\ast}=\|a\|_{p^\ast}
    \ge m^{-1/p}\|a\|_1
    = 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:rho-profile}{\normalcolor \rho_{p,q,d}\IfBlankF{def:rho-profile}{(def:rho-profile)}}} 
[m]\|a\|_1.
\]

If \(q<2\), choose an orthonormal basis
\(u_1,\dots,u_m\in\mathbb R^m\) with
\[
    \|u_i\|_\infty\lesssim m^{-1/2}
\]
and embed it in the first \(m\) coordinates of \(\mathbb R^d\). Set
\(
    g_i:= c_qm^{1/q-1/2}u_i.
\)
Then \(g_i\in B_{q^\ast}^d\), and, since \(p^\ast\ge q^\ast>2\),
\[
    \|Ta\|_{p^\ast}
    \gtrsim_{p,q}
    m^{1/q-1/2}m^{1/p^\ast-1/2}\|a\|_2
    \ge
    m^{-(1/p+1/2-1/q)}\|a\|_1
    =
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:rho-profile}{\normalcolor \rho_{p,q,d}\IfBlankF{def:rho-profile}{(def:rho-profile)}}} 
[m]\|a\|_1.
\]

\@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
   {1.5ex plus 0.5ex minus .2ex} 
   {-1em} 
   {\normalfont\normalsize\rmfamily\bfseries} 
{The range \(q<p\).}
If \(q>2\), partition a subset of \([d]\) into \(m\) disjoint blocks
\(B_1,\dots,B_m\) of common size \(b\asymp d/m\), and set
\(
    g_i:= b^{-1/q^\ast}\mathbf 1_{B_i}.
\)
Then \(\|g_i\|_{q^\ast}=1\), and the disjoint supports give
\[
    \|Ta\|_{p^\ast}
    =b^{1/q-1/p}\|a\|_{p^\ast}
    \ge
    b^{1/q-1/p}m^{-1/p}\|a\|_1
    \asymp
    d^{1/q-1/p}m^{-1/q}\|a\|_1.
\]

Finally, suppose \(q\le2\). Kashin's random-sign construction
\citep{kashin1977diameters} gives, for \(m\le c_pd\), a matrix
\(G\in\{\pm1\}^{d\times m}\) such that
\[
    \|Ga\|_{p^\ast}\gtrsim_p d^{1/p^\ast}\|a\|_2,
    \qquad a\in\mathbb R^m.
\]
For \(p^\ast\le2\), this is the Kashin \(\ell_1\)-estimate followed by norm
comparison. For \(p^\ast\ge2\), it follows from the smallest singular-value
bound and the comparison of \(\ell_{p^\ast}\) with \(\ell_2\). If \(G_i\)
is column \(i\), define
\(
    g_i:= d^{-1/q^\ast}G_i.
\)
Then \(\|g_i\|_{q^\ast}=1\), and
\[
    \|Ta\|_{p^\ast}
    =d^{-1/q^\ast}\|Ga\|_{p^\ast}
    \gtrsim_{p,q}
    d^{1/q-1/p}m^{-1/2}\|a\|_1.
\]
In each case the support-function criterion gives the asserted cube.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{prop:sobolev-function-space-example}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:prop:sobolev-function-space-example}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        \llap{\smash{ 
        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
            \setlength\@tempdima\HyperRaiseLinkDefault
            \HyperRaiseLinkHook
        \expandafter\endgroup
        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{ 
            \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
            \hypertarget{proof:prop:sobolev-function-space-example}{} 
            \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor
        } 
        }} 
        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
W e first prove the fat-shattering lower bound. 
By the Rellich-Kondrachov compact embedding theorem, the inclusion $J:H^s(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L_2(\Omega)$
is compact. Since both spaces are Hilbert and $J$ is injective, the singular-value decomposition for compact Hilbert-space operators gives an orthonormal basis $(e_j)_{j\ge1}$ of $H^s(\Omega)$, an orthonormal basis $(\phi_j)_{j\ge1}$ of $L_2(\Omega)$, and positive weights $(w_j)_{j\ge1}$ satisfying $Je_j=w_j^{-1}\phi_j$. 
Thus, if $u=\sum_{j\ge1}a_je_j\in H^s(\Omega)$, then, viewing $u$ as an element of $L_2(\Omega)$,  $ u=Ju=\sum_{j\ge1}a_jw_j^{-1}\phi_j$.  Writing $\theta_j=a_jw_j^{-1}$ gives us $\|u\|_{H^s(\Omega)}^2
    =
    \sum_{j\ge1}a_j^2
    =
    \sum_{j\ge1}w_j^2\theta_j^2$.  
Hence, we have the following representation 
\begin{equation*}
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 

    =
    \bigg\{
        \sum_{j\ge1}\theta_j\phi_j:
        \sum_{j\ge1}w_j^2\theta_j^2\le1
    \bigg\}.
\end{equation*}
The classical spectral estimate for the Sobolev embedding gives $w_j\asymp_{s,m}j^{s/m}$,
see \citet[Section~3.3.4]{edmunds1996function}.\footnote{In the notation of \citet{edmunds1996function}, take $A=F$, $p_1=p_2=q_1=q_2=2$, $s_1=s$, $s_2=0$, and dimension $m$. Their approximation-number estimate then gives $a_j\asymp_{s,m}j^{-s/m}$. For compact operators between Hilbert spaces, approximation numbers coincide with singular values, while in our decomposition the corresponding singular value is $w_j^{-1}$.} Let $n$ satisfy $\frac{\alpha^2}{4}\sum_{j=1}^n w_j^2\le1$, and take $g_j=\phi_j \in  
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$, $j=1,\ldots,n$, with zero thresholds. For every
$\varepsilon\in\{\pm1\}^n$, define $u_\varepsilon
    :=
    \frac{\alpha}{2}
    \sum_{j=1}^n\varepsilon_j\phi_j$.
Then $\sum_{j\ge1}w_j^2
    \langle u_\varepsilon,\phi_j\rangle_{L_2}^2
    =
    \frac{\alpha^2}{4}\sum_{j=1}^n w_j^2
    \le1$, 
so $u_\varepsilon\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$, while for every $j\le n$, $\varepsilon_j\langle g_j,u_\varepsilon\rangle
    =
    \frac{\alpha}{2}$.
Thus $g_1,\ldots,g_n$ are $\alpha$-fat-shattered. Since $w_j\asymp_{s,m}j^{s/m}$, the standard integral comparison for sums of powers gives
\begin{align*}
    \sum_{j=1}^n w_j^2 \asymp_{s,m}
    \sum_{j=1}^n j^{2s/m} \asymp_{s,m}
    \int_0^n x^{2s/m}\,dx=
    \frac{1}{1+2s/m}\,
    n^{1+2s/m} \asymp_{s,m}
    n^{1+2s/m}.
\end{align*}
Therefore the condition on $n$, and the fact that we can have a set of size $n$ that is fat-shattered, implies $ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])
    \gtrsim_{s,m}
    \alpha^{-\frac{2m}{m+2s}}$.
\medskip


 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
For the sequential upper bound, it is sufficient to construct a uniform cover of the linear class, since every uniform cover induces a sequential cover on any input tree. For $f_u(g)=\langle g,u\rangle$ and $f_v(g)=\langle g,v\rangle$, since $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
=B_{L_2(\Omega)}$, we have $\|f_u-f_v\|_\infty=\sup_{g\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}|\langle g,u-v\rangle|=\|u-v\|_{L_2}$. Thus a uniform cover of $ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]$ is equivalent to an $L_2$-cover of $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$.
Let $N_2( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,R)$ denote the covering number of $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$ by radius-$R$ balls in $L_2(\Omega)$. The Sobolev entropy estimate of \citet[Sections~3.3.2 and~3.5]{edmunds1996function} gives
\[
    \log N_2( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,R)\lesssim_{s,m}R^{-m/s},\qquad 0<R<1.
\]
Cover $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$ by these balls.
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

 
Note that the squared $L^2$ distance to the center of each ball is $(1/4,2)$-uniformly convex  
     
     
         
     
     
     
with range at most $R^2$ (see \cref{def:uniform-convexity-and-range} for the notions of uniform convexity and range) \citep{clarkson1936uniformly}. Hence, for any $R \in (0,1)$, \cref{lem:uniformly-convex-localization,lem:seq_union_bound} gives
\begin{equation*}
     
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])
    \lesssim_{s,m} \inf_{R \in (0,1)}
    \left\{\frac{R^2}{\alpha^2}
    +
    \log N_2( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,R)\right\} \lesssim_{s,m} \inf_{R \in (0,1)} \left\{\frac{R^2}{\alpha^2}+R^{-m/s}\right\} \lesssim_{s,m}
    \alpha^{-\frac{2m}{m+2s}},
\end{equation*}
where the last inequality follows by taking $R=\alpha^{2s/(m+2s)}$ for $0<\alpha<1$, so both terms equal $\alpha^{-2m/(m+2s)}$.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{prop:lipschitz_bound}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:prop:lipschitz_bound}{} 
    \else

        \begingroup

            \HyperRaiseLinkHook

        \expandafter\raise\the\@tempdima\hbox{

        \Hy@RestoreSpaceFactor
        \penalty\@M\hskip\z@
    \fi
F or the ordinary fat-shattering lower bound, take a grid
$x_1,\ldots,x_n\in[1/2,1]^m$ (this is basically the elements of $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$) with spacing $\alpha$ in each coordinate. Then
$n\asymp_m\alpha^{-m}$ and, for every $i\neq j$,
$\|x_i-x_j\|_\infty\ge\alpha$. For every
$\varepsilon\in\{\pm1\}^n$, let $u_\varepsilon(0)=0$ and
$u_\varepsilon(x_i)=\varepsilon_i\alpha/2$. These values are $1$-Lipschitz on
$\{0,x_1,\ldots,x_n\}$, since for $i\neq j$, $|u_\varepsilon(x_i)-u_\varepsilon(x_j)|
    \le
    \alpha
    \le
    \|x_i-x_j\|_\infty$,
while
$|u_\varepsilon(x_i)-u_\varepsilon(0)|=\alpha/2\le\|x_i\|_\infty$.
By the McShane extension theorem, $u_\varepsilon$ extends to a
$1$-Lipschitz function on $\Omega$. Since $g_i=\delta_{x_i}$, $\varepsilon_i\langle \delta_{x_i},u_\varepsilon\rangle
    =
    \varepsilon_i u_\varepsilon(x_i)
    =
    \frac{\alpha}{2}$.
Hence $x_1,\ldots,x_n$ are
$\alpha$-fat-shattered with zero thresholds, and therefore $ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{fat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])
    \gtrsim_m
    \alpha^{-m}$. \medskip



For the sequential upper bound, the entropy estimate for Lipschitz functions gives $\log {\protect\hyperlink{def:covering-number}{\normalcolor N_\infty}}( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,\alpha)
    \lesssim_m
    \alpha^{-m}$, 
see \citet[Theorem 2.7.1.]{vanderVaart1996}. Since $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
=\{0\}\cup\{\pm\delta_x:x\in\Omega\}$, the uniform metric on $ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
]$ is exactly the uniform metric on $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$, as $\sup_{g\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
}
    |\langle g,u-v\rangle|
    =
    \sup_{x\in\Omega}
    |u(x)-v(x)|
    =
    \|u-v\|_\infty$.


Now suppose a tree of depth $n$ is $\alpha$-shattered, and let
$u_\varepsilon\in 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$ be a witness for each path
$\varepsilon\in\{\pm1\}^n$. If two distinct paths $\varepsilon,\varepsilon'$ first
differ at level $t$, they encounter the same point $x_t$ and threshold
$r_t$. Assuming without loss of generality that $\varepsilon_t=1$ and
$\varepsilon_t'=-1$, the shattering inequalities give $u_\varepsilon(x_t)\ge r_t+\frac{\alpha}{2}$, $u_{\varepsilon'}(x_t)\le r_t-\frac{\alpha}{2}$, 
and hence
$\|u_\varepsilon-u_{\varepsilon'}\|_\infty\ge\alpha$.
Thus the $2^n$ witnesses are pairwise $\alpha$-separated, so by triangle inequality any uniform
$\alpha/4$-cover of $ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
$ contains at least $2^n$ elements. Therefore
\begin{equation*}
    n\log2
    \le
    \log {\protect\hyperlink{def:covering-number}{\normalcolor N_\infty}}( 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
,\alpha/4)
    \lesssim_m
    \alpha^{-m}.
\end{equation*}
Hence
$ 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:sequential-fat-shattering-dimension}{\normalcolor \operatorname{sfat}\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[\alpha]( 
     
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:linear-pairing-class}{\normalcolor \mathcal L\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
[#1][#2] 
[ 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:feasible-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal X\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
, 
    {\protect\hyperlink{def:subgradient-set}{\normalcolor \mathcal H\IfBlankF{#3}{_{#3}}\IfBlankF{#4}{^{#4}}}} 
])\lesssim_m\alpha^{-m}$.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
\textbf{of \cref{prop:basis_bound}. } 
    \ifvmode
        \hypertarget{proof:prop:basis_bound}{} 
    \else
        \Hy@SaveSpaceFactor

        \begingroup
            \let\@tempdima\@tempdima
